# DocMind M5 (Erasure) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** After M5, a signed-in user can call `DELETE /api/me` (confirming their password) and their account is erased:
- their running work is stopped;
- the user's data key is destroyed, so every encrypted record of theirs (event log, snapshots, sealed columns, reasoning, blobs) becomes permanently unreadable;
- their rows are hard-deleted;
- their blobs are deleted.

Erasure is durable and idempotent: an interrupted erasure resumes at boot, and re-running it is harmless. An end-to-end test proves nothing of the user stays decryptable or readable, and that the server still boots and serves other users afterwards.

This covers spec §9 item 34 and the erasure carries listed in the README.

**Architecture:** An `Eraser` service in `apps/server/app/erasure/`, driven by a durable `erasure_requests` row. It runs fixed, idempotent steps in order and records each completed step on the row. The route only authenticates, confirms the password, logs the user out everywhere, inserts the request and answers 202; the eraser does the rest in the background. A boot pass resumes unfinished requests.

The crypto shred is Loom's `app.eraseSubject(userId, tenant, {parked: "shred"})`. That needs DocMind to:
- wire `compliance.erase.shred` to `shredSubject` over the raw stores;
- rebuild Loom's in-memory subject index at boot;
- make read-model catch-up tolerate shredded logs.

**Tech Stack:** everything through M4, plus `@loom/compliance` (`shredSubject`, subject index) via `@loom/app`'s `compliance` config. Loom stays pinned at `58fb2f5c`; nothing here needs a Loom change.

**Spec:** `/Users/such/workspace/docmind/docs/specs/2026-10-18-docmind-design.md`: §4.1–§4.4 (esp. "Crypto-shredding", "Erasure flow", "Known trade-offs") and §9 item 34.

**Research (read it):**
- `/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-such-workspace/2760bbed-302e-49dd-8fc8-8f5a0a6085ce/scratchpad/m5-research/loom-erasure.md`: Loom erasure APIs with file:line.
- `/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-such-workspace/2760bbed-302e-49dd-8fc8-8f5a0a6085ce/scratchpad/m5-research/docmind-inventory.md`: every store of a user's data, cascades and roles.

## Findings that shape this plan (verified 2026-10-20, DocMind main f660870, Loom 58fb2f5c)

| # | Finding | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| E1 | `buildApp` has no `compliance` block, so `app.eraseSubject` is unusable. | Task 164 wires `compliance: {subjectOf: subjectOfExecution, erase: {shred: shredSubject over durable.raw stores}}`. |
| E2 | `eraseSubject` calls the shred, which destroys the key itself (`KeyStore.destroy`), writes `ERASURE_PENDING`, then `PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` tombstones on each of the subject's executions. Spec steps 1 and 4 therefore collapse: the key is gone after step 1. | Order (D3): stop work, shred (key destroyed), purge read models, hard-delete rows, delete blobs, re-assert `keys.destroy` (idempotent), done. DocMind's own sealed columns become unreadable at step 1 and are then hard-deleted. |
| E3 | "Live" = non-terminal. A chat parked on its inbox is live, so a default erase defers forever. `{parked: "shred"}` cancels suspended owners with reason "erased" and shreds. A **running** owner still defers. | D4: the eraser stops work first, calls `eraseSubject` with `{parked: "shred"}`, and retries with backoff while the outcome is `parked`. |
| E4 | Loom's subject index is fed live by `onEvent` only, and is empty after a restart. `eraseSubject` then returns `unknown` and writes no tombstones. | Task 164 hydrates the index at boot by scanning `raw.eventStore.listExecutionsPage` for `u_<uuid>` executions (sequence 0), as Loom's CLI does (`tools/cli/src/compliance.ts:46-56`). |
| E5 | `ReadModelService.catchUp` has no try/catch, and a strict `EncryptingEventStore` throws `PayloadShreddedError` on a shredded payload. A model behind a shredded execution, or any rebuild or version bump, would refuse boot. | Task 165: read-model catch-up reads through a **tolerant** source (`onShredded: "tolerate"` → `{$shredded: true}`), and every folded handler skips shredded events. A boot test erases a user, then restarts, then rebuilds. |
| E6 | `DELETE FROM users` cascades to auth_sessions, documents (and so chunks, parsed_documents, chunk_links, chunk_entities), entities and chat_sessions (and so chat_turn_items, chat_reasoning_texts). These do NOT cascade: `read_model_state`, `auth_throttle`, `data_keys`, `loom.*`. `docmind_app` cannot delete tenants (0003), and has SELECT only on `read_model_state`. | Read-model rows are tombstoned with `purgeFolded` through the `docmind_loom` writer. `auth_throttle` (keyed by a hash of the email) is cleared by the `DELETE /api/me` route with `throttle_clear` before the request transaction replaces the email (D1b). The personal tenant is deleted by a new narrow SECURITY DEFINER function (D6). |
| E7 | `purgeFolded` is exact-match and tombstones only. Ingest-status rows for chat and title executions have a NULL scope. | Task 167 tombstones every read-model row whose `execution_id` starts with `u_<user>` (both models), plus by scope. |
| E8 | `TimerStore` has no prefix API; Loom retires timers of terminal executions. | After the shred, the eraser deletes leftover timer and lease rows for `u_<user>…` on the loom pool (`starts_with`). Events and snapshots stay as tombstones and ciphertext (D7). |
| E9 | `eraseUserBlobs(root, tenant, user)` exists, is idempotent, and is used only by tests. | The eraser calls it, and calls it again after the key destroy to catch an upload racing the erase (D4 step 7). |
| E10 | `GET /api/documents/events` never re-checks the session (M4 follow-up). Neither SSE route can close one user's streams. | Task 166: `closeUser(userId)` on both stream route groups, plus the heartbeat re-auth from M4's chat stream ported to the documents stream. |

## Decisions

- **D1. The route.**
  - `DELETE /api/me` is behind `requireAuth` and the origin guard.
  - Body `{password}` (contract `DeleteAccountRequest`). The password is verified with the login hasher, with the same throttle as login (a wrong password → 401 `invalid_credentials`, throttled).
  - On success, in one transaction, it:
    - inserts `erasure_requests (user_id, tenant_id, receipt_hash, state 'pending')`;
    - deletes all of the user's `auth_sessions` (logged out everywhere);
    - sets `users.erasing_at = now()`;
    - **scrubs the email**: `users.email = 'erased:' || user_id`.
  - It then answers **202** `{erasure: "accepted", receipt: <token>}`, clears the session cookie, and starts the eraser in the background.
  - **The receipt (D1a).** The token is 32 random bytes from `node:crypto`, base64url (43 characters). Only `receipt_hash = sha256(token text)` is stored (`bytea NOT NULL UNIQUE`); the token itself is never stored or logged. It is the only handle the caller has once the session is gone.
  - **The status route (D1a).** `GET /api/erasure/:receipt` is unauthenticated. A token of the wrong shape (not base64url, not 43 characters) is 404 without touching the database. Otherwise the route hashes the token and calls `docmind_erasure_status(p_receipt_hash bytea) RETURNS text` (SECURITY DEFINER, STRICT, `search_path` pinned, REVOKE PUBLIC, GRANT EXECUTE to `docmind_app`), which takes a hash and returns only a state: `'pending'`, `'done'` or NULL. Answers: 200 `{state}`, or 404 (unknown token and bad shape look the same). It is throttled per IP with the existing pgThrottle pattern (every request counts, then `retryAfter`; 429 with `Retry-After`) and every answer carries `Cache-Control: no-store`.
  - **The email scrub (D1b).** The app role can already `UPDATE users` under RLS: `0002` gives `users` one `tenant_isolation` policy (`USING (tenant_id = app_tenant_id())`, which also serves as `WITH CHECK`), `0001`'s default privileges grant `UPDATE`, and no later migration revokes it on `users`. So the scrub is one more statement in the route's own tenant transaction and needs no new function and no change to `0024` beyond `receipt_hash`. `'erased:' || user_id` is unique (so `users.email UNIQUE` holds) and is not a valid address (the `Email` contract cannot produce it), so nobody can sign up or log in as it. The `rows` step deletes the row, placeholder included.
  - **Consequences of the scrub.** Login can no longer match the old email, and the `erasing_at` refusal in `auth_find_login` and `auth_find_session` stays as a second safeguard (it covers a row marked by anything other than this route). **Sign-up with the same email succeeds immediately**: the address is free from the moment of the 202, so a new user and tenant are created even while the old erasure is pending. The old email is in no table after the 202. The login and sign-up throttle counters of the email (keyed by a hash of it) are cleared by the route, because the `rows` step can no longer read the email.
  - The password check is `AuthService.confirmPassword` (a new method in `packages/auth`: "right password and this exact account"; `AuthStore` is unchanged).
  - Login and session lookup refuse a user whose `erasing_at` is set, in the database (`auth_find_login` and `auth_find_session` are replaced in `0024`), with the same answer as invalid credentials.
- **D2. Durable request (migration `0024`).**
  - **The table.** `erasure_requests (user_id uuid PK, tenant_id uuid, receipt_hash bytea NOT NULL UNIQUE (32 bytes), state text CHECK IN ('pending','done'), steps text[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}', attempts int, requested_at, updated_at, completed_at)`.
    - No FK to users, because the row must outlive the user.
    - It holds ids, a receipt hash, step names and times only, no email or content.
    - **RLS is on** (the tenancy suite requires it on every public table), with an INSERT-only policy for `docmind_app`: the check pins the row to the transaction's tenant, to a user of that tenant, and to a fresh pending request with no steps. `REVOKE ALL`, then `GRANT INSERT`. Without that check a forged insert could name another tenant's user. The app role reads and changes the table only through three SECURITY DEFINER functions: `docmind_erasure_pending(p_state text DEFAULT 'pending')` returns the requests in that state (ids only, at most 1000; `p_state` is validated to `pending|done`, STRICT), `docmind_erasure_mark(user_id, step, done bool)` (step `'retry'` counts an attempt), and `docmind_erasure_status(p_receipt_hash bytea) RETURNS text` (STRICT; takes a hash and returns only a state, `'pending'`, `'done'` or NULL, which is what the unauthenticated receipt route needs). Each function has `search_path` pinned, REVOKE PUBLIC, and GRANT EXECUTE to docmind_app.
    - This is a **fourth cross-tenant path**, ruled explicitly (open question 3).
  - **The column.** `users.erasing_at timestamptz NULL`.
  - **The auth functions.** `0024` replaces `auth_find_login` and `auth_find_session` (same signatures, so grants carry over): both refuse a user whose `erasing_at` is set.
  - **The tenant.** `docmind_delete_empty_tenant(tenant_id)` (SECURITY DEFINER) deletes a tenant only if it has no users left.
  - **Definer count.** Four erasure functions (`docmind_erasure_pending`, `docmind_erasure_mark`, `docmind_erasure_status`, `docmind_delete_empty_tenant`) plus the sweep's two make **six** `docmind_` definer functions, and stay six: `erased()` reuses `docmind_erasure_pending('done')`, and the email scrub is a plain `UPDATE` under the caller's own RLS (D1b), so neither adds a seventh.
- **D3. Steps, in order.** Each step is idempotent, recorded in `steps` when done, and skipped when it is already recorded.
  1. `stop`: close the user's SSE streams, cancel every ingest generation of each document, stop and close every chat (`stops.stop`, `conversations.close`), cancel chat-title executions, and settle the StartScheduler for the user.
  2. `shred`: `app.eraseSubject(userId, undefined, {parked: "shred"})` (the tenant is **undefined**: DocMind has no `compliance.tenantOf`, so Loom's subject index has one partition and a tenant id would answer `unknown` and write no tombstones; the port is `ErasureDeps.eraseSubject(userId)`), retried with backoff (1 s, 2 s, 4 s … at most 60 s, unbounded attempts) while the outcome is `parked`. `unknown` (no executions) is fine. After `erased`, assert `keys.wasDestroyed(userId)`.
  3. `readModels`: tombstone ingest-status and chat-activity rows by scope = user, and by execution-id prefix `u_<user>`.
  4. `rows`:
     - `DELETE FROM users` (cascade; the email is already scrubbed, and the throttle counters were cleared by the route);
     - delete leftover loom `timers` and `leases` rows by prefix (loom pool);
     - `docmind_delete_empty_tenant`.
  5. `blobs`: `eraseUserBlobs(root, tenant, user)`.
  6. `key`: `keys.destroy(userId)` again (idempotent), assert `wasDestroyed`, and **re-run `blobs`** (E9: the second blob sweep comes after the key destroy, to catch an upload racing the erase).
  7. `done`: state `done`, `completed_at`.
- **D4. Running the eraser.**
  - `Eraser.run(userId)` is single-flight per user, through an in-process map plus a Postgres advisory lock `erasure:<user>`.
  - The route fires it. Boot resumes every `pending` request after recovery has started.
  - Errors log the step name and the error class only, then retry with backoff (`ErasureStore.retried` counts each failed attempt). A step never runs out of order.
  - **Stuck warning (B).** `ERASURE_STUCK_AFTER = 10`. When one step has failed 10 times in a row (this process's attempt counter for that step), the eraser logs one line, `erasure stuck <step> <ErrorClass> attempts=<n>`, and again at every 10 further attempts (20, 30, ...), never on the attempts in between. The line never contains an id, an email or an error message. A shred parked behind a long chat turn reaches it too (class `ErasureParkedError`). The counter restarts when the process restarts.
  - `Eraser.stop()` ends every backoff wait, lets a running step finish (bounded) and starts no new step. Shutdown needs it because `idle()` would wait on a backoff. `closeSequence` gains a first step, `stopErasure`, so the eraser finishes before the ingest port and the chat close.
  - `Eraser.resumeAll()` starts every pending request, then calls `ErasureStore.erased()` (required) and re-runs `forgetUser` for each done user after the read models have booted, because a rebuild can re-create an erased execution's row as `{}`.
  - Test seams: `ComposeOptions.erasure = { loomPool, wrap?, sleep? }` (`wrap` lets the crash test make a step fail; `sleep` caps the backoff).
- **D5. Boot safety (E5).**
  - Read-model catch-up (ingest-status and chat-activity) uses a tolerant decrypting source; the live path stays strict.
  - Folded handlers skip events whose payload is `{$shredded: true}`, or whose execution has a `PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` tombstone, without changing state.
  - A version bump re-folds over shredded logs without throwing. Versions: `ingest-status` goes `"3"` to `"4"` and `chat-activity` `"2"` to `"3"`, so every deployment re-folds both models once at the first boot after the merge (say so in the PR body).
- **D6. Personal tenant.** It is deleted when its last user is erased. Multi-user tenants stay; there is no tenant-level erasure UI in v1.
- **D7. What stays, by design.** It is documented in the spec as a known trade-off.
  - In Loom's log: the execution ids (`u_<user>:…`, plaintext UUIDs), event types, timestamps and the shred tombstones. Payloads are ciphertext under a destroyed key.
  - In DocMind: the `erasure_requests` audit row, which holds ids and times only.
  - In `data_keys`: a tombstone row (subject id and `destroyed_at`, no key material), by design of migration 0007. The proof asserts it separately from the exhaustive scan.
  - In backups: residue until the backups expire, which is 30 days by policy.
- **D8. The proof** (the item 34 test). It runs end to end on Postgres with fake Ollama and FakeAnthropic.
  - **Before erasure:** a user signs up, uploads a document (to `ready`), and chats with reasoning, a citation and an auto title. A second user in the same tenant does the same.
  - **After `DELETE /api/me` completes, every one of these holds:**
    - zero rows naming the user id in every public table except `erasure_requests`, `read_model_state`, `read_model_folds` and `data_keys`, which have their own narrower assertions (checked by an exhaustive query over `information_schema` columns of type uuid, text, varchar and jsonb);
    - only tombstoned `read_model_state` rows, with state NULL;
    - no blob directory;
    - `keys.wasDestroyed` is true;
    - every raw `loom.events` payload of the user's executions is either plaintext metadata or an envelope that fails to open;
    - a read through the sealed store throws `PayloadShreddedError` or yields `$shredded`;
    - no timers or leases.
  - The other user's chat, history, reasoning and documents still work.
  - Immediately after the 202, before the eraser has run: the old email appears in no table, login with the old credentials fails, the receipt reads `pending`, and signing up with the same email works and creates a fresh account that sees nothing. After the eraser finishes, the receipt reads `done`, and an unknown or malformed receipt is 404.
  - Restarting the server twice (catch-up plus a forced read-model rebuild) boots cleanly.
  - Re-running the erasure is a no-op.
  - Killing the server between steps (after `shred`, before `rows`) and restarting resumes and completes.

## Deviations recorded from the first draft of this header

These are binding where they differ from the decisions above as first written; the decisions above already reflect them.

- **Tenant `undefined` (E2/D3).** `eraseSubject(userId, undefined, {parked: "shred"})`; a tenant id silently answers `unknown`. If a later task wants a tenant partition, `tenantOf` must be added to `complianceConfig` and the hydration must feed it.
- **RLS on `erasure_requests` (D2).** Not "RLS is not applied": on, with the INSERT-only policy.
- **`auth_find_*` refusal in `0024` (D1/D2).** Login and session refusal of an erasing user live in SQL; Task 170a supplies the route, the cookie and the tests. With the email scrub (D1b) it is a second safeguard, not the first.
- **Receipt, scrub and sixth function (D1a/D1b/D2).** `erasure_requests.receipt_hash`, `docmind_erasure_status`, `GET /api/erasure/:receipt` (Task 170b), and `users.email` rewritten to `erased:<user id>` in the request transaction (Task 170a). The `rows` step no longer touches the throttle: the route clears the email's counters instead. The earlier ruling "sign-up of an erasing email returns 202 and creates nothing" is withdrawn.
- **`forgetUser` returns `Promise<number>`** (rows newly tombstoned), not `Promise<void>`: `IngestStatus.forgetUser` already does and its tests pin it; `ChatActivity.forgetUser` matches. It throws synchronously on a bad id. Prefix purging is a lister on the Loom pool plus `purgeFolded({ executionId })`, because Loom's `FoldedSelector` has no prefix form.
- **Model versions:** ingest-status `"4"`, chat-activity `"3"` (D5).
- **`ErasureStore.retried(userId)`** is new (`ErasureRequest.attempts` needs a writer); it goes through `docmind_erasure_mark(user, 'retry', false)`, so the footprint stays four erasure functions (with `docmind_erasure_status`).
- **`ErasureStore.get(userId)` returns pending only**: a done request is `null`, which makes `Eraser.run` a no-op on a re-run. `insert`'s `tx` is typed `Queryable`, not `PoolClient`.
- **`ErasureStore.erased()` is required** and returns `Promise<string[]>` (done users, newest first, at most 1000), backed by `docmind_erasure_pending('done')`.
- **Eraser additions:** `Eraser.stop()` and the `stopErasure` close step; `ComposeOptions.erasure` test seams; `ErasureDeps.eraseSubject(userId)`; the `key` step re-runs blobs; `AuthService.confirmPassword`; the stuck warning (`ERASURE_STUCK_AFTER`, `ErasureDeps.log`).
- **`ErasureStore` additions:** `insert(tx, userId, tenantId, receiptHash)` and `status(receiptHash)`.
- **Not done, by design:** `shredSubject`'s `reason` is not set (the erasure request is the audit record, D7); `hydrateSubjectIndex` runs once at boot (executions created afterwards reach the index through the live `onEvent`).

## Open questions (defaults are what the tasks implement)

1. **D1, password confirmation:** required. *Alternative: no re-auth, since the session and origin guard suffice.*
2. **D1, async 202:** erasure completes in the background, because a running chat turn can defer the shred. *Alternative: wait synchronously up to N seconds.*
3. **D2, a fourth cross-tenant path:** three narrow SECURITY DEFINER functions over `erasure_requests` (ids and states only; `docmind_erasure_pending` takes a state, `docmind_erasure_status` takes a receipt hash), plus the tenant function, needed to resume erasures at boot and to answer the receipt route without a tenant context. *Alternative: store tenant-scoped requests under RLS and resume them by iterating tenants with a definer listing function, which is the same footprint.*
4. **D6, deleting the personal tenant** when its last user goes.
5. **D7, keep Loom tombstones and execution ids** rather than deleting executions outright. They are needed for Loom's own consistency and are plaintext ids only.
6. **D1b, the email is freed at the 202** (scrubbed to `erased:<user id>`), so a re-sign-up works at once. *Alternative: keep the email until the `rows` step and make sign-up of it a silent no-op, which is what the first draft ruled; rejected because the user could not start over until the eraser finished, and the address sat in `users` for as long as a running turn held the shred.*
7. **D1a, the receipt route is unauthenticated** and answers with a state only. The token is the credential (256 bits); the route is throttled per IP and 404s unknown tokens.

## Global Constraints

Everything in the M4 plan's Global Constraints still binds (PR size, R6, stacked drafts, rebase-merge, commit trailer, the local gate with `DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL` exported in the same command, test hygiene, the never-log list, tenancy, encryption, migration rules). Additions:

- **The erasure step names and the user id are the only things the eraser may log.** It logs step name, error class and attempt (and the stuck line `erasure stuck <step> <ErrorClass> attempts=<n>`), and never a receipt, an email, title, filename, document id beside content, key or connection string.
- **Migrations:** the next free number is `0024`.
- **Cross-tenant paths after M5 are exactly four:** Loom recovery; the read-model writer (`docmind_loom`); the sweep's two definer functions; and M5's erasure definer functions (`docmind_erasure_pending(p_state)`, `docmind_erasure_mark`, `docmind_erasure_status(p_receipt_hash)`, `docmind_delete_empty_tenant`). Counted by name with the sweep's two, six definer functions. Each is STRICT where it applies, has `search_path` pinned, returns ids or a state only (`docmind_erasure_status` takes a hash and returns only a state), has REVOKE PUBLIC and GRANT EXECUTE to docmind_app.
- **Dependency rules:** `@loom/compliance` may be added to apps/server through the root `pnpm.overrides`, like the other `@loom/*` packages.

## Interface catalogue (binding names)

```ts
// apps/server/app/erasure/ (Tasks 164–172)
export const ERASURE_STEPS = ["stop", "shred", "readModels", "rows", "blobs", "key", "done"] as const;   // erasure/types.ts
export type ErasureStep = (typeof ERASURE_STEPS)[number];
export const isErasureStep: (x: unknown) => x is ErasureStep;
export interface ErasureRequest { userId: string; tenantId: string; state: "pending" | "done"; steps: ErasureStep[]; attempts: number }
export interface Queryable { query(text: string, values?: unknown[]): Promise<unknown> }
export type ErasureState = "pending" | "done";
export interface ErasureStore {                                                                          // Task 168
  insert(tx: Queryable, userId: string, tenantId: string, receiptHash: Buffer): Promise<void>;   // idempotent
  status(receiptHash: Buffer): Promise<ErasureState | null>;                // docmind_erasure_status(hash): 'pending' | 'done' | null
  pending(): Promise<ErasureRequest[]>;                                     // docmind_erasure_pending('pending')
  erased(): Promise<string[]>;                                              // REQUIRED. docmind_erasure_pending('done'): done user ids, newest first, <= 1000
  mark(userId: string, step: ErasureStep): Promise<void>;                   // 'done' also finishes the request; throws if no such request
  retried(userId: string): Promise<void>;                                   // counts a failed attempt (docmind_erasure_mark(user,'retry',false))
  get(userId: string): Promise<ErasureRequest | null>;                      // PENDING only; a done request is null
}
export class PgErasureStore implements ErasureStore { constructor(pool: Pool) }                          // erasure/pgStore.ts
export class MemoryErasureStore implements ErasureStore {}                                               // erasure/testing.ts (also noSnapshots)
export function newReceipt(): { token: string; hash: Buffer };  export function hashReceipt(token: string): Buffer;   // erasure/receipt.ts: 32 random bytes, base64url; sha256 of the token text
export const ERASURE_STUCK_AFTER = 10;                                                                   // erasure/eraser.ts (Task 169a); ErasureDeps.log?(line: string)
// Task 169: a flat set of narrow functions, one per collaborator, so each step is testable alone.
export interface ErasureDeps { store: Pick<ErasureStore, "pending" | "erased" | "mark" | "retried" | "get">; lock(userId): Promise<(() => Promise<void>) | null>; work(req); closeStreams(userId); eraseSubject(userId: string): Promise<{ outcome: "erased" | "parked" | "unknown" }>; keys; forgetStatus(userId); forgetActivity(userId); /* abridged: the full shape (rows, blobs, sleep, log) is defined in Task 169a */ }
export class Eraser { constructor(deps: ErasureDeps); run(userId: string): Promise<void>; resumeAll(): Promise<void>; idle(): Promise<void>; stop(): Promise<void> }
export const STEP_ORDER: readonly ErasureStep[];  export function backoffMs(attempt: number): number;  export function abortableSleep(ms: number, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<void>;
export interface ComposeOptions { erasure?: { loomPool: Pool; wrap?: (d: ErasureDeps) => ErasureDeps; sleep?: ErasureDeps["sleep"] } }   // buildServer returns eraser?: Eraser
export interface CloseDeps { stopErasure?(): Promise<unknown> }   // first step of closeSequence
export interface AccountErasure { request(identity, receiptHash: Buffer): Promise<void>; start(userId: string): void; status(receiptHash: Buffer): Promise<ErasureState | null> /* 170b */ }  export function pgAccountErasure(o: { pool; store; eraser: Pick<Eraser, "run"> }): AccountErasure;  // Task 170
AuthService.confirmPassword(...)                                                                         // packages/auth, Task 170a
// contracts (packages/contracts/src/auth.ts): DeleteAccountRequest, DeleteAccountResponse {erasure, receipt}, ErasureReceipt (170a); ErasureStatusResponse {state} (170b)
// authRoutes: DELETE /api/me (170a), GET /api/erasure/:receipt (170b, unauthenticated, throttled, no-store)
export function hydrateSubjectIndex(raw: EventStore, index: SubjectIndex): Promise<number>;              // Task 164
export function tolerantSource(...): EventStore;                                                         // Task 165
// stream route groups gain: closeUser(userId: string): void                                             // Task 166
// IngestStatus / ChatActivity gain: forgetUser(userId: string): Promise<number>  (rows newly tombstoned; scope + execution-id prefix) // Task 167
// startIngestStack({ wrapErasure?: (d: ErasureDeps) => ErasureDeps }); IngestStack.eraser: Eraser        // Task 171
```

---

## Phase A: making erasure possible

The tasks of this phase change nothing a user can see. They make `app.eraseSubject` usable (Task 164), make the read models survive an erased log (165), give the two SSE route groups a per-user close (166), give both read models a complete `forgetUser` (167), and add the durable request table and its store (168). Task 169 onwards builds the `Eraser` on top of them.

---

### Task 164: Compliance wiring: `compliance.erase.shred`, and the subject index hydrated at boot

Implements finding E1 and E4. `buildApp` gets a `compliance` block (it has none, so `app.eraseSubject` rejects today), and the boot rebuilds Loom's in-memory subject index from the log before anything is served.

Two facts shape the code:

- **`compliance` is a top-level key of `defineConfig`** (Loom `packages/app/src/defineConfig.ts:297`), not a child of `security`. The shape is `{ subjectOf, tenantOf?, erase?: { shred, destroyed? } }`.
- **`subjectOf` only needs the execution id.** DocMind already names the erasure subject from the id alone (`subjectOfExecution`, `u_<user>[:…|.c-…]`). So the resolver ignores the payload, and the hydrate step does not have to read or decrypt any event. It feeds the index one synthetic bootstrap envelope per `u_…` execution. Reading sequence 0 as Loom's CLI does (`tools/cli/src/compliance.ts:46-56`) would fail on a compacted log; this cannot.

DocMind sets no `tenantOf`, so the index has a single partition and **`app.eraseSubject(userId, undefined, …)` must pass no tenant**. A user id is a UUID, unique across tenants. Passing a tenant id here would query a partition that does not exist and answer `unknown` (see "Deviations recorded" in the header).

**Files:**
- Modify: `package.json` (root: `pnpm.overrides`), `apps/server/package.json` (dependency)
- Create: `apps/server/app/erasure/compliance.ts`, `apps/server/app/erasure/testing.ts`
- Modify: `apps/server/app/config.ts` (`FlowDeps.erasure`, the `compliance` block)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/compose.ts` (`ComposeOptions.erasure`, passed to `buildApp`)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/bootIngest.ts` (hydrate after the models boot)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/setup.ts` (`erasure: { raw: durable.raw }`)
- Test: `apps/server/app/erasure/compliance.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/erasure/wiring.pg.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/config.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/bootIngest.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - M1: `subjectOfExecution`, `executionIdFor` (`subject.ts`).
  - `createDurableStorage(...).raw` (`storage.ts`): the unwrapped event and snapshot stores. `shredSubject` must write its tombstones to the raw log.
  - Loom: `SubjectIndex`, `Shredder` (`@loom/compliance`); `shredSubject`, `ShredOptions`, `KeyStore` (`@loom/plugin-crypto`); `EventStore.listExecutionsPage` (cursor is strictly-after, ids in code-point order: `COLLATE "C"`).
- Produces:
  - `interface ErasureWiring { keys: KeyStore; raw: { eventStore: EventStore; snapshotStore: NonNullable<ShredOptions["snapshotStore"]> } }`.
  - `userSubjectOf(e: { executionId: string }): string | undefined` (the `SubjectResolver`: the user for a user-owned execution, `undefined` for any other).
  - `userShredder(wiring: ErasureWiring): Shredder` (idempotent; `shredSubject` over the raw stores).
  - `complianceConfig(wiring: ErasureWiring)` (the object `buildApp` passes as `compliance`).
  - `hydrateSubjectIndex(raw, index, pageSize?): Promise<number>` (the number of user executions attributed).
  - `FlowDeps.erasure?: ErasureWiring`; `ComposeOptions.erasure?: { raw: ErasureWiring["raw"] }`.
  - `noSnapshots` in `erasure/testing.ts`: a snapshot store stub that has no snapshots, for tests over an in-memory event store.

```ts
import type { ErasureWiring } from "./compliance";

/**
 * A snapshot store with no snapshots, for tests whose event store is in memory. `shredSubject` refuses
 * to run over a store that can `truncatePrefix` without one, and only ever reads it.
```

`apps/server/app/erasure/compliance.test.ts`:

```ts
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { SubjectIndex } from "@loom/compliance";
import type { EventEnvelope } from "@loom/core";
import { InMemoryAsyncEventStore } from "@loom/event-runtime";
import { InMemoryKeyStore } from "@loom/plugin-crypto";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { chatConversationId } from "../chats/ids";
import { executionIdFor } from "../subject";
import { complianceConfig, hydrateSubjectIndex, userShredder, userSubjectOf } from "./compliance";
import { noSnapshots } from "./testing";

const [A, B] = [randomUUID(), randomUUID()];
const CHAT = randomUUID();

const ev = (
  executionId: string,
  sequence: number,
  eventType: string,
  payload: unknown = {},
): EventEnvelope => ({
  id: `${executionId}-${sequence}`,
  workflowId: "wf",
  executionId,
  eventType,
  sequence,
  timestamp: 1_000 + sequence,
  payload,
});
const subjectOf = (e: EventEnvelope) => userSubjectOf(e);
const index = () => new SubjectIndex(subjectOf);

/** A store that can only list: hydration must not need to read (or decrypt) a single event. */
const listOnly = (ids: readonly string[]) => {
  const sorted = [...ids].sort((x, y) => (x < y ? -1 : x > y ? 1 : 0));
  return {
    async listExecutionsPage(opts?: { cursor?: string; limit?: number }) {
      const after = opts?.cursor === undefined ? sorted : sorted.filter((id) => id > opts.cursor!);
      const limit = opts?.limit ?? 1000;
      const executionIds = after.slice(0, limit);
      return {
        executionIds,
        ...(after.length > limit && { nextCursor: executionIds[executionIds.length - 1] as string }),
      };
    },
  };
};

describe("userSubjectOf", () => {
  it("is the user for every execution the user owns", () => {
    for (const executionId of [
      executionIdFor(A, "ingest-x"),
      executionIdFor(A, "chat-title-y"),
      chatConversationId(A, CHAT),
      `${chatConversationId(A, CHAT)}~3`,
      `${executionIdFor(A, "ingest-x")}::step#0`,
    ])
      expect(userSubjectOf({ executionId })).toBe(A);
  });

  it("is undefined for an execution no user owns, so the index never groups it", () => {
    for (const executionId of ["system-sweep", `u_not-a-uuid:a`, `x_${A}:a`, A, "__loom_erasure__"])
      expect(userSubjectOf({ executionId })).toBeUndefined();
  });
});

describe("hydrateSubjectIndex", () => {
  const ids = [
    executionIdFor(A, "ingest-1"),
    executionIdFor(A, "ingest-1.2"),
    executionIdFor(A, "chat-title-1"),
    chatConversationId(A, CHAT),
    `${chatConversationId(A, CHAT)}~1`,
    executionIdFor(B, "ingest-1"),
    "system-sweep",
    "__loom_erasure__",
    "zz-after-the-user-range",
  ];

  it("attributes every user-owned execution to its user and nothing else", async () => {
    const idx = index();
    expect(await hydrateSubjectIndex(listOnly(ids), idx)).toBe(6);
    expect(idx.executionsOf(A)).toEqual(ids.slice(0, 5).sort());
    expect(idx.executionsOf(B)).toEqual([executionIdFor(B, "ingest-1")]);
    expect(idx.subjects()).toEqual([A, B].sort());
  });

  it("pages: a page size of 2 finds the same executions", async () => {
    const idx = index();
    expect(await hydrateSubjectIndex(listOnly(ids), idx, 2)).toBe(6);
    expect(idx.executionsOf(A)).toHaveLength(5);
  });

  it("is idempotent, and merges with what the live feed already indexed", async () => {
    const idx = index();
    idx.handleEvent(ev(executionIdFor(A, "ingest-1"), 0, "EXECUTION_STARTED"));
    await hydrateSubjectIndex(listOnly(ids), idx);
    await hydrateSubjectIndex(listOnly(ids), idx);
    expect(idx.executionsOf(A)).toHaveLength(5);
  });

  it("is a no-op for a store with no user executions", async () => {
    const idx = index();
    expect(await hydrateSubjectIndex(listOnly(["system-sweep"]), idx)).toBe(0);
    expect(idx.size).toBe(0);
  });

  it("refuses a store that cannot list executions: erasure would silently find nothing", async () => {
    await expect(hydrateSubjectIndex({}, index())).rejects.toThrow(
      /cannot list executions/,
    );
  });
});

describe("userShredder", () => {
  it("destroys the user's key and tombstones every named execution on the raw log", async () => {
    const keys = new InMemoryKeyStore();
    const raw = new InMemoryAsyncEventStore();
    const [e1, e2] = [executionIdFor(A, "ingest-1"), chatConversationId(A, CHAT)];
    for (const id of [e1, e2]) {
      await raw.append(ev(id, 0, "EXECUTION_STARTED"));
      await raw.append(ev(id, 1, "EXECUTION_COMPLETED"));
    }
    await keys.getOrCreate(A);
    const shred = userShredder({ keys, raw: { eventStore: raw, snapshotStore: noSnapshots } });

    await shred(A, [e1, e2]);
    expect(await keys.wasDestroyed(A)).toBe(true);
    for (const id of [e1, e2])
      expect((await raw.read(id)).map((e) => e.eventType)).toEqual([
        "EXECUTION_STARTED",
        "EXECUTION_COMPLETED",
        "ERASURE_PENDING",
        "PAYLOAD_SHREDDED",
      ]);

    await shred(A, [e1, e2]); // idempotent: Loom calls it again on a retry
    expect((await raw.read(e1)).filter((e) => e.eventType === "PAYLOAD_SHREDDED")).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  it("does nothing for an empty execution list", async () => {
    const keys = new InMemoryKeyStore();
    const shred = userShredder({
      keys,
      raw: { eventStore: new InMemoryAsyncEventStore(), snapshotStore: noSnapshots },
    });
    await shred(A, []);
    expect(await keys.wasDestroyed(A)).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe("complianceConfig", () => {
  it("names the resolver and the shredder, and takes the destruction proof from the key store", async () => {
    const keys = new InMemoryKeyStore();
    const cfg = complianceConfig({
      keys,
      raw: { eventStore: new InMemoryAsyncEventStore(), snapshotStore: noSnapshots },
    });
    expect(cfg.subjectOf).toBe(userSubjectOf);
    expect(typeof cfg.erase.shred).toBe("function");
    expect(await cfg.erase.destroyed?.(A)).toBe(false);
    await keys.destroy(A);
    expect(await cfg.erase.destroyed?.(A)).toBe(true);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `erasure/compliance.ts`.**

```ts
import type { Shredder, SubjectIndex } from "@loom/compliance";
import type { EventEnvelope } from "@loom/core";
import type { EventStore } from "@loom/event-runtime";
import { type KeyStore, type ShredOptions, shredSubject } from "@loom/plugin-crypto";
import { subjectOfExecution } from "../subject";

/** What erasure needs from the storage layer: the key store, and the raw (unwrapped) event and snapshot stores. */
export interface ErasureWiring {
  keys: KeyStore;
  raw: {
    eventStore: EventStore;
    snapshotStore: NonNullable<ShredOptions["snapshotStore"]>;
  };
}

/**
 * Loom's `SubjectResolver`: the user an execution belongs to, or `undefined` for an execution no user
 * owns (`subjectOfExecution` keys those as `x:<id>`, outside the user namespace; they are not data
 * subjects and must never be grouped into an erasure). Only the execution id is read, so it works on
 * a payload that is sealed or shredded.
 */
export function userSubjectOf(e: { executionId: string }): string | undefined {
  const subject = subjectOfExecution(e);
  return subject.startsWith("x:") ? undefined : subject;
}

/**
 * Loom's `Shredder`: destroys the user's key and appends `ERASURE_PENDING` and `PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` to
 * each execution's log. It must write to the RAW store (a tombstone sealed under the destroyed key
 * would be unreadable), and be given the raw snapshot store so the tombstone's sequence clears any
 * checkpoint. Idempotent: `shredSubject` skips an execution that already has its tombstone.
 */
export function userShredder({ keys, raw }: ErasureWiring): Shredder {
  return async (subjectId, executionIds, options) => {
    const first = executionIds[0];
    if (first === undefined) return; // Loom answers "unknown" before it gets here
    await shredSubject(keys, raw.eventStore, first, {
      subjectId,
      executionIds: [...executionIds],
      snapshotStore: raw.snapshotStore,
      ...(options?.force && { force: options.force }),
    });
  };
}

/**
 * The `compliance` block of `defineConfig`. `destroyed` is the proof Loom needs to finish an erasure
 * that was interrupted after the key was destroyed: the key store's own durable `wasDestroyed`. It is
 * passed explicitly (Loom would also find it through `security.crypto`) so the proof does not depend
 * on how the storage was wired. No `tenantOf`: user ids are UUIDs, unique across tenants, so one
 * partition is enough and `eraseSubject` is called without a tenant.
 */
export function complianceConfig({ keys, raw }: ErasureWiring) {
  const proof = keys.wasDestroyed?.bind(keys);
  return {
    subjectOf: userSubjectOf,
    erase: {
      shred: userShredder({ keys, raw }),
      ...(proof && { destroyed: proof }),
    },
  };
}

/** User-owned execution ids all start with this; the log lists ids in code-point order, so they are one contiguous run. */
const USER_PREFIX = "u_";
export const HYDRATE_PAGE = 500;

const bootstrapOf = (executionId: string): EventEnvelope => ({
  id: `hydrate-${executionId}`,
  workflowId: "",
  executionId,
  eventType: "EXECUTION_STARTED",
  sequence: 0,
  timestamp: 0,
  payload: {},
});

/**
 * Rebuilds Loom's subject index after a restart (it is fed live by `onEvent` only, so a fresh process
 * has none of the executions it did not start, and `eraseSubject` would answer `unknown` and shred
 * nothing: finding E4). Lists the `u_…` executions, one page at a time, and hands the index a synthetic
 * bootstrap event for each. The resolver reads the id alone, so no event is read and nothing is
 * decrypted; a log whose first events were compacted away hydrates the same. Safe to call again and
 * alongside the live feed. Returns how many user executions it attributed.
 */
export async function hydrateSubjectIndex(
  raw: Pick<EventStore, "listExecutionsPage">,
  index: Pick<SubjectIndex, "handleEvent">,
  pageSize = HYDRATE_PAGE,
): Promise<number> {
  if (raw.listExecutionsPage === undefined)
    throw new TypeError("hydrateSubjectIndex: the event store cannot list executions");
  let attributed = 0;
  let cursor: string = USER_PREFIX; // the cursor is strictly-after: every `u_…` id sorts after `u_`
  for (;;) {
    const page = await raw.listExecutionsPage({ cursor, limit: pageSize });
    for (const executionId of page.executionIds) {
      if (!executionId.startsWith(USER_PREFIX)) return attributed; // past the user range
      if (userSubjectOf({ executionId }) === undefined) continue; // `u_` but not a user execution id
      index.handleEvent(bootstrapOf(executionId));
      attributed += 1;
    }
    if (page.nextCursor === undefined) return attributed;
    cursor = page.nextCursor;
  }
}
```

```ts
  // erasure would answer "unknown" and shred nothing. Rebuild it from the log before anything is served. A failure
  // here is fatal: a server that cannot erase must not take requests (the error is the fixed label, never an id).
```

```ts
import { createSecretKey, randomBytes, randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { PostgresKeyStore } from "@docmind/keystore-pg";
import { PayloadShreddedError } from "@loom/plugin-crypto";
import { Pool } from "pg";
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { buildApp } from "../config";
import { migrate, setAppRolePassword, setRolePassword } from "../db/migrate";
import { APP_PASSWORD, LOOM_PASSWORD, PG_URL, type TestDb, createTestDb } from "../db/testSupport";
import { type DurableStorage, createDurableStorage } from "../storage";
import { executionIdFor } from "../subject";
import { hydrateSubjectIndex } from "./compliance";

describe.skipIf(!PG_URL)("erasure wiring over durable storage (needs DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL)", () => {
  let db: TestDb;
  let pool: Pool;
  let keys: PostgresKeyStore;
  let stores: DurableStorage;
  const [A, B] = [randomUUID(), randomUUID()];
  const [a1, a2, b1] = [
    executionIdFor(A, "ingest-1"),
    `u_${A}.c-${randomUUID()}`,
    executionIdFor(B, "ingest-1"),
  ];

  beforeAll(async () => {
    db = await createTestDb();
    await migrate(db.owner);
    await setAppRolePassword(db.owner, APP_PASSWORD);
    await setRolePassword(db.owner, "docmind_loom", LOOM_PASSWORD);
    pool = new Pool({ connectionString: db.appUrl });
    keys = new PostgresKeyStore(pool, [{ id: "kek-1", key: createSecretKey(randomBytes(32)) }]);
    stores = createDurableStorage({ loomUrl: db.loomUrl, keys });
    stores.crypto.events.registerSensitive("NOTE_ADDED", ["text"]);
    // Executions are written straight to the store, as a previous process would have left them:
    // nothing reaches `onEvent`, so a freshly built app's subject index is empty.
    for (const [id, text] of [
      [a1, "a secret"],
      [a2, "a chat secret"],
      [b1, "b secret"],
    ] as const) {
      const at = (sequence: number, eventType: string, payload: unknown) => ({
        id: randomUUID(),
        workflowId: "notes",
        executionId: id,
        eventType,
        sequence,
        timestamp: 1_000 + sequence,
        payload,
      });
      await stores.sealed.eventStore.append(at(0, "EXECUTION_STARTED", { input: {} }));
      await stores.sealed.eventStore.append(at(1, "NOTE_ADDED", { text }));
      await stores.sealed.eventStore.append(at(2, "EXECUTION_COMPLETED", {}));
    }
  });

  afterAll(async () => {
    await stores?.storage.eventStore.close?.();
    await stores?.close();
    await pool?.end();
    await db?.drop();
  });

  it("answers unknown from an empty index and shreds nothing; once hydrated it erases, and only that user", async () => {
    const app = buildApp(stores.storage, stores.crypto, {
      erasure: { keys, raw: stores.raw },
    });
    try {
      const index = app.subjectIndex;
      if (!index) throw new Error("the app has no subject index");
      expect(index.executionsOf(A)).toEqual([]);
      await expect(app.eraseSubject(A, undefined, { parked: "shred" })).resolves.toMatchObject({
        outcome: "unknown",
        erased: [],
      });
      expect(await keys.wasDestroyed(A)).toBe(false);

      expect(await hydrateSubjectIndex(stores.raw.eventStore, index)).toBe(3);
      const result = await app.eraseSubject(A, undefined, { parked: "shred" });
      expect(result).toMatchObject({ subjectId: A, outcome: "erased" });
      expect([...result.erased].sort()).toEqual([a1, a2].sort());

      expect(await keys.wasDestroyed(A)).toBe(true);
      await expect(stores.sealed.eventStore.read(a1)).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(PayloadShreddedError);
      expect((await stores.raw.eventStore.read(a2)).at(-1)?.eventType).toBe("PAYLOAD_SHREDDED");
      // The other user is untouched.
      expect((await stores.sealed.eventStore.read(b1))[1]?.payload).toEqual({ text: "b secret" });
      expect(await keys.wasDestroyed(B)).toBe(false);
    } finally {
      await app.close();
    }
  });

  it("is idempotent: erasing the same user again changes nothing and does not throw", async () => {
    const app = buildApp(stores.storage, stores.crypto, { erasure: { keys, raw: stores.raw } });
    try {
      const index = app.subjectIndex;
      if (!index) throw new Error("the app has no subject index");
      await hydrateSubjectIndex(stores.raw.eventStore, index);
      const again = await app.eraseSubject(A, undefined, { parked: "shred" });
      expect(again.outcome).toBe("erased");
      expect(
        (await stores.raw.eventStore.read(a1)).filter((e) => e.eventType === "PAYLOAD_SHREDDED"),
      ).toHaveLength(1);
    } finally {
      await app.close();
    }
  });
});
```

### Task 165: Read models tolerate an erased log

Implements finding E5 and D5. A shredded log read through the strict `EncryptingEventStore` throws `PayloadShreddedError`, and `ReadModelService.catchUp` has no `try/catch` around the read. So a model that is behind an erased execution (a restart after an erase, a new model, a version bump, a rebuild) refuses to boot. Two changes:

1. **A tolerant source.** A second `EncryptingEventStore` over the same raw store and key store, with `onShredded: "tolerate"`, reads an erased user's sealed fields as `{ $shredded: true }`. Both models fold through it (live gap-fill and catch-up). The runtime, the chat reads and every authoritative read keep the strict store. It must never be handed to the runtime (Loom: a resume off a placeholder would diverge).
2. **Handlers that fold a tombstone to a content-free state.** The two handler sets already skip a `{ $shredded: true }` payload (`payloadOf`). They also get a `PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` handler that returns the neutral state `{}`. A row that a rebuild un-tombstones for an erased execution then holds no status and no counts. Both versions are bumped, so the next boot re-folds once.

The tolerant store takes its sensitive map from the strict store's `sealingCapability().covers` after `defineConfig` has run, because `defineConfig` registers the principal paths on the strict instance after construction and decryption is driven by the map. Copying the construction-time map would miss them.

**Files:**
- Create: `apps/server/app/erasure/tolerantSource.ts`
- Modify: `apps/server/app/compose.ts` (the models' `source`)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/status/model.ts` (`PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` handler; version `"4"`)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/chats/activity.ts` (`PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` handler; version `"3"`)
- Test: `apps/server/app/erasure/tolerantSource.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/status/model.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/chats/activity.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/erasure/bootAfterErase.pg.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - Task 164: `ComposeOptions.erasure`, `userShredder`, `noSnapshots`.
  - Loom: `EncryptingEventStore` (`onShredded`, `sealingCapability`), `isShredded`, `PayloadShreddedError` (`@loom/plugin-crypto`); `isKeyUnavailable` (`@loom/event-runtime`); `ReadModelService.registerFolded/catchUp`.
- Produces:
  - `tolerantSource({ raw, keys, strict }): EventStore` and `sensitiveFromCovers(covers): Record<string, string[]>`.
  - `ingestStatus.version === "4"`, `chatActivity.version === "3"`; both handler sets include `PAYLOAD_SHREDDED`.

```ts
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import type { EventEnvelope } from "@loom/core";
import { InMemoryAsyncEventStore, isKeyUnavailable } from "@loom/event-runtime";
import {
  EncryptingEventStore,
  InMemoryKeyStore,
  PayloadShreddedError,
  isShredded,
} from "@loom/plugin-crypto";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { executionIdFor, subjectOfExecution } from "../subject";
import { userShredder } from "./compliance";
import { noSnapshots } from "./testing";
import { sensitiveFromCovers, tolerantSource } from "./tolerantSource";

const U = randomUUID();
const EXEC = executionIdFor(U, "ingest-1");
const ev = (sequence: number, eventType: string, payload: unknown): EventEnvelope => ({
  id: `${EXEC}-${sequence}`,
  workflowId: "wf",
  executionId: EXEC,
  eventType,
  sequence,
  timestamp: 1_000 + sequence,
  payload,
});

function setup() {
  const raw = new InMemoryAsyncEventStore();
  const keys = new InMemoryKeyStore();
  const strict = new EncryptingEventStore(raw, keys, {
    sensitive: { NOTE_ADDED: ["text"] },
    subjectOf: subjectOfExecution,
  });
  // Registered after construction, the way `defineConfig` registers the principal paths.
  strict.registerSensitive("OTHER_ADDED", ["body"]);
  return { raw, keys, strict };
}

describe("sensitiveFromCovers", () => {
  it("rebuilds the per-event-type path map from `EVENT_TYPE.path` covers", () => {
    expect(
      sensitiveFromCovers(["A.x", "A.y.z", "B.q[].r", "malformed", ".alsoBad"]),
    ).toEqual({ A: ["x", "y.z"], B: ["q[].r"] });
  });
});

describe("tolerantSource", () => {
  it("reads an erased user's sealed fields as placeholders, where the strict store throws", async () => {
    const { raw, keys, strict } = setup();
    await strict.append(ev(0, "EXECUTION_STARTED", { input: {} }));
    await strict.append(ev(1, "NOTE_ADDED", { text: "secret", kept: "plain" }));
    await strict.append(ev(2, "OTHER_ADDED", { body: "also secret" }));
    const tolerant = tolerantSource({ raw, keys, strict });
    expect((await tolerant.read(EXEC))[1]?.payload).toEqual({ text: "secret", kept: "plain" });

    await userShredder({ keys, raw: { eventStore: raw, snapshotStore: noSnapshots } })(U, [EXEC]);

    await expect(strict.read(EXEC)).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(PayloadShreddedError);
    const events = await tolerant.read(EXEC);
    const payloadOf = (i: number) => events[i]?.payload as Record<string, unknown>;
    expect(isShredded(payloadOf(1).text)).toBe(true);
    expect(payloadOf(1).kept).toBe("plain");
    expect(isShredded(payloadOf(2).body)).toBe(true); // a path registered after construction is covered
    expect(events.at(-1)?.eventType).toBe("PAYLOAD_SHREDDED");
    expect((await tolerant.readFrom(EXEC, 2)).length).toBe(3);
  });

  it("is not a way around a missing key: a node without the key, and no tombstone, still refuses", async () => {
    const { raw, keys, strict } = setup();
    await strict.append(ev(0, "NOTE_ADDED", { text: "secret" }));
    const elsewhere = tolerantSource({ raw, keys: new InMemoryKeyStore(), strict });
    await expect(elsewhere.read(EXEC)).rejects.toSatisfy(isKeyUnavailable);
    void keys;
  });

  it("is a reader: it lists executions like the strict store", async () => {
    const { raw, keys, strict } = setup();
    await strict.append(ev(0, "EXECUTION_STARTED", { input: {} }));
    const tolerant = tolerantSource({ raw, keys, strict });
    expect(await tolerant.listExecutionsPage?.({ limit: 10 })).toEqual({ executionIds: [EXEC] });
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3 (165a): Write `erasure/tolerantSource.ts`.**

```ts
import type { EventStore } from "@loom/event-runtime";
import { EncryptingEventStore, type KeyStore } from "@loom/plugin-crypto";
import { subjectOfExecution } from "../subject";

/** `["EVENT_TYPE.path", …]` (a store's `sealingCapability().covers`) back into the per-type path map. */
export function sensitiveFromCovers(covers: readonly string[]): Record<string, string[]> {
  const sensitive: Record<string, string[]> = {};
  for (const cover of covers) {
    const dot = cover.indexOf(".");
    if (dot <= 0 || dot === cover.length - 1) continue; // an event type has no dot; ignore a malformed entry
    (sensitive[cover.slice(0, dot)] ??= []).push(cover.slice(dot + 1));
  }
  return sensitive;
}

export interface TolerantSourceOptions {
  /** The raw (unwrapped) event store the strict one wraps. */
  raw: EventStore;
  keys: KeyStore;
  /** The strict sealing store, read for what it seals once `defineConfig` has registered its framework paths. */
  strict: Pick<EventStore, "sealingCapability">;
}

/**
 * A read-only view of the log for the read models: an erased user's sealed fields read as
 * `{ $shredded: true }` instead of throwing `PayloadShreddedError`, so catch-up, a rebuild and a version
 * bump can fold past an erased execution. It redacts only a real shred (a `PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` tombstone
 * in the log): a key that is merely missing on this node still raises `KeyUnavailableError`.
 *
 * Never hand it to the runtime, to a resume or to any read that returns data to a user: a fold off a
 * placeholder is not the original, so the authoritative path keeps the strict store.
 */
export function tolerantSource({ raw, keys, strict }: TolerantSourceOptions): EventStore {
  return new EncryptingEventStore(raw, keys, {
    sensitive: sensitiveFromCovers(strict.sealingCapability?.()?.covers ?? []),
    subjectOf: subjectOfExecution,
    onShredded: "tolerate",
  });
}
```


#### 165b: the boot over an erased user, on Postgres

- [ ] **Step 7 (165b): Write the Postgres test (`erasure/bootAfterErase.pg.test.ts`).** It proves E5 on the real stores: the strict source refuses, the tolerant one boots, the erased row holds nothing, the other user's row is intact, a restart is a no-op, and a version bump re-folds over the erased log.

```ts
import { createSecretKey, randomBytes, randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { PostgresKeyStore } from "@docmind/keystore-pg";
import { RECORD_SOURCE } from "@loom/conversation";
import { type EventEnvelope, projection$ } from "@loom/core";
import { PayloadShreddedError, isShredded } from "@loom/plugin-crypto";
import { ReadModelService } from "@loom/plugin-read-model";
import { Pool } from "pg";
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import {
  CHAT_ACTIVITY_MODEL,
  ChatActivity,
  type ChatActivityState,
  INITIAL_ACTIVITY,
  activityHandlers,
  chatKeyOf,
  chatScopeOf,
} from "../chats/activity";
import { chatConversationId } from "../chats/ids";
import { migrate, setAppRolePassword, setRolePassword } from "../db/migrate";
import { APP_PASSWORD, LOOM_PASSWORD, PG_URL, type TestDb, createTestDb } from "../db/testSupport";
import { pgStatusWriter } from "../status/pgStores";
import { flowSteps, statusSensitive } from "../status/wire";
import { type DurableStorage, createDurableStorage } from "../storage";
import { userShredder } from "./compliance";
import { tolerantSource } from "./tolerantSource";

describe.skipIf(!PG_URL)("read-model boot over an erased user (needs DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL)", () => {
  let db: TestDb;
  let pool: Pool;
  let keys: PostgresKeyStore;
  let durable: DurableStorage;
  const [A, B] = [randomUUID(), randomUUID()];
  const [chatA, chatB] = [chatConversationId(A, randomUUID()), chatConversationId(B, randomUUID())];

  const event = (
    executionId: string,
    sequence: number,
    eventType: string,
    payload: unknown,
  ): EventEnvelope => ({
    id: randomUUID(),
    workflowId: "doc-chat",
    executionId,
    eventType,
    sequence,
    timestamp: 1_000 + sequence,
    payload,
  });
  const record = (id: string, sequence: number, role: "user" | "reply", text: string) =>
    event(id, sequence, "DECISION_RECORDED", {
      decisionId: `conv#o1:${role}`,
      source: RECORD_SOURCE,
      choice: { turn: 1, role: role === "reply" ? "assistant" : "user", status: "complete", ord: 1, text },
    });

  const writer = () => pgStatusWriter(durable.pool);
  const sensitive = () => statusSensitive(flowSteps(), durable.sealed.eventStore);
  const fold = () =>
    tolerantSource({ raw: durable.raw.eventStore, keys, strict: durable.sealed.eventStore });
  const activityRows = async () =>
    (
      await db.owner.query<{ execution_id: string; scope: string | null; state: ChatActivityState | null; erased: boolean }>(
        "SELECT execution_id, scope, state, erased FROM read_model_state WHERE model_id = $1 ORDER BY execution_id",
        [CHAT_ACTIVITY_MODEL],
      )
    ).rows;

  beforeAll(async () => {
    db = await createTestDb();
    await migrate(db.owner);
    await setAppRolePassword(db.owner, APP_PASSWORD);
    await setRolePassword(db.owner, "docmind_loom", LOOM_PASSWORD);
    pool = new Pool({ connectionString: db.appUrl });
    keys = new PostgresKeyStore(pool, [{ id: "kek-1", key: createSecretKey(randomBytes(32)) }]);
    durable = createDurableStorage({ loomUrl: db.loomUrl, keys });
    for (const [id, who] of [
      [chatA, "a"],
      [chatB, "b"],
    ] as const) {
      const append = (e: EventEnvelope) => durable.sealed.eventStore.append(e);
      await append(event(id, 0, "EXECUTION_STARTED", { input: {} }));
      await append(record(id, 1, "user", `${who} asked something private`));
      await append(record(id, 2, "reply", `${who} was told something private`));
      await append(event(id, 3, "EXECUTION_COMPLETED", {}));
    }
    // User A is erased BEFORE any read model has seen the log (a node that was down, or a new model).
    await userShredder({ keys, raw: durable.raw })(A, [chatA]);
  });

  afterAll(async () => {
    await durable?.storage.eventStore.close?.();
    await durable?.close();
    await pool?.end();
    await db?.drop();
  });

  it("the strict and the tolerant source differ exactly where the log is erased", async () => {
    await expect(durable.sealed.eventStore.read(chatA)).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(PayloadShreddedError);
    const tolerant = await fold().read(chatA);
    const choice = (tolerant[1]?.payload as { choice: { text: unknown; role: string } }).choice;
    expect(isShredded(choice.text)).toBe(true);
    expect(choice.role).toBe("user"); // only the sealed path is replaced
    expect((await durable.sealed.eventStore.read(chatB))[1]?.payload).toMatchObject({
      choice: { text: "b asked something private" },
    });
  });

  it("a model behind an erased execution cannot boot through the strict source (E5)", async () => {
    const strict = new ChatActivity({
      writer: writer(),
      source: durable.sealed.eventStore,
      sensitive: sensitive(),
      onError: () => {},
    });
    await expect(strict.boot()).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(PayloadShreddedError);
  });

  it("boots through the tolerant source: the erased execution's row holds no counts, the other user's is intact", async () => {
    const activity = new ChatActivity({
      writer: writer(),
      source: fold(),
      sensitive: sensitive(),
      onError: () => {},
    });
    await activity.boot();
    await activity.close();
    const rows = await activityRows();
    expect(rows.map((r) => r.execution_id).sort()).toEqual([chatA, chatB].sort());
    expect(rows.find((r) => r.execution_id === chatA)).toMatchObject({ scope: A, state: {} });
    expect(rows.find((r) => r.execution_id === chatB)).toMatchObject({
      scope: B,
      state: { n: 2, o: 1 },
    });
  });

  it("a restart is a no-op, and so is a second one", async () => {
    for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) {
      const activity = new ChatActivity({
        writer: writer(),
        source: fold(),
        sensitive: sensitive(),
        onError: () => {},
      });
      await activity.boot();
      await activity.close();
    }
    expect(await activityRows()).toHaveLength(2);
  });

  it("a version bump re-folds the whole log, erased user included, without throwing", async () => {
    const bumped = projection$<ChatActivityState>({
      id: CHAT_ACTIVITY_MODEL,
      name: "ChatActivity",
      version: "999",
      initialState: INITIAL_ACTIVITY,
      handlers: activityHandlers,
    });
    const service = new ReadModelService(writer(), { sensitive: sensitive(), onError: () => {} });
    const { rebuilt } = await service.registerFolded({
      projection: bumped,
      keyOf: chatKeyOf,
      scopeOf: chatScopeOf,
    });
    expect(rebuilt).toBe(true);
    await service.catchUp([fold()]);
    const rows = await activityRows();
    expect(rows.find((r) => r.execution_id === chatA)?.state).toEqual({});
    expect(rows.find((r) => r.execution_id === chatB)?.state).toMatchObject({ n: 2 });
  });
});
```

### Task 166: `closeUser` on both SSE route groups, and the heartbeat re-auth on the documents stream

Implements finding E10. Erasure has to end the user's open streams: a stream outlives its login otherwise, and the progress bus keeps delivering to a user whose rows are going away. Today each route group has only a global `closeAll`. The documents stream also never re-checks the session (an M4 carry), so one that the erasure missed would stay open until the client dropped it. The chat stream already re-authenticates every heartbeat.

Two mechanisms, deliberately together:

- `closeUser(userId)` ends every open stream of one user at once, frees their slots, and (unlike `closeAll`) leaves the group open for everyone else and for that user's later requests.
- The documents stream's heartbeat re-checks the session and ends the stream if it is gone, as the chat stream does. This covers a request that passed authentication in the moment before `closeUser` ran.

**Files:**
- Modify: `apps/server/app/documents/events.ts` (`openStream` re-check; `closeUser`)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/chats/stream.ts` (`closeUser`)
- Test: `apps/server/app/documents/eventsClose.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/chats/streamCloseUser.pg.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - M3/M4: `documentEventRoutes`, `openStream`, `chatStreamRoutes`, `Schedule`, `requireAuth`; test kit `openSse`, `testAuthenticator`, `U1`, `U1B`, `ALICE`, `ALICE2` (`documents/testHarness.ts`); `startChatServer`, `seedChat` (`chats/testHarness.ts`).
- Produces:
  - `documentEventRoutes(...)` returns `{ routes, closeAll(): void, closeUser(userId: string): void }`.
  - `chatStreamRoutes(...)` returns `{ routes, closeAll(): void, closeUser(userId: string): void }`.
  - `openStream(res, onClose, bus, scope, schedule, heartbeatMs, recheck?)`: the optional `recheck(): Promise<boolean>` runs at each heartbeat; `false` or a rejection ends the stream (fail closed).

`apps/server/app/documents/eventsClose.test.ts`:

```ts
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import type { AddressInfo } from "node:net";
import type { Authenticator } from "@docmind/auth";
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { createHttpServer } from "../server";
import { documentEventRoutes } from "./events";
import { InMemoryProgressBus } from "./progress";
import { U1, U1B, openSse, testAuthenticator } from "./testHarness";

const ev = () => ({ documentId: randomUUID(), step: "chunk", status: "parsing" }) as const;

async function start(
  over: { authenticator?: Authenticator } = {},
) {
  const bus = new InMemoryProgressBus();
  let beat: () => void = () => {};
  let ended: () => void = () => {};
  const events = documentEventRoutes({
    authenticator: over.authenticator ?? testAuthenticator,
    bus,
    maxStreamsPerUser: 2,
    schedule: (fn) => {
      beat = fn;
      return () => {};
    },
    onStreamEnd: () => ended(),
  });
  const server = createHttpServer(events.routes);
  await new Promise<void>((resolve) => server.listen(0, "127.0.0.1", resolve));
  const base = `http://127.0.0.1:${(server.address() as AddressInfo).port}`;
  return {
    bus,
    events,
    beat: () => beat(),
    nextEnd: () =>
      new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
        ended = resolve;
      }),
    open: (user: string) => openSse(`${base}/api/documents/events`, { "x-test-user": user }),
    async stop() {
      events.closeAll();
      server.closeAllConnections();
      await new Promise<void>((resolve) => server.close(() => resolve()));
    },
  };
}

let current: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof start>> | undefined;
afterEach(async () => {
  await current?.stop();
  current = undefined;
});

describe("closeUser", () => {
  it("ends every stream of that user at once and frees their slots, and no other user's", async () => {
    const t = (current = await start());
    const [a1, a2] = [await t.open("alice"), await t.open("alice")];
    const other = await t.open("alice2");
    for (const s of [a1, a2, other]) expect(await s.next()).toBe("retry: 5000");

    const gone = t.nextEnd();
    t.events.closeUser(U1);
    await gone;
    for (const s of [a1, a2]) await expect(s.next()).rejects.toThrow(/^stream ended$/);

    const mine = ev();
    t.bus.publish({ userId: U1B }, mine);
    expect(await other.next()).toContain(mine.documentId); // the other user's stream is still attached
    other.close();
  });

  it("is not a shutdown: the group still accepts the user's next stream, and the cap is free again", async () => {
    const t = (current = await start());
    const [a1, a2] = [await t.open("alice"), await t.open("alice")];
    await a1.next();
    await a2.next();
    t.events.closeUser(U1);
    const again = await t.open("alice");
    expect(again.res.status).toBe(200);
    expect(await again.next()).toBe("retry: 5000");
    again.close();
  });

  it("does nothing for a user with no stream", async () => {
    const t = (current = await start());
    expect(() => t.events.closeUser(randomUUID())).not.toThrow();
  });
});

describe("heartbeat re-authentication", () => {
  const revocable = () => {
    const state = { revoked: false, fails: false };
    const authenticator: Authenticator = {
      async authenticate(req) {
        if (state.fails && state.revoked) throw new Error("db down");
        return state.revoked ? null : testAuthenticator.authenticate(req);
      },
    };
    return { state, authenticator };
  };

  it("keeps a stream whose session is still valid", async () => {
    const { authenticator } = revocable();
    const t = (current = await start({ authenticator }));
    const s = await t.open("alice");
    await s.next();
    t.beat();
    expect(await s.next()).toBe(": ping");
    const e = ev();
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 20));
    t.bus.publish({ userId: U1 }, e);
    expect(await s.next()).toContain(e.documentId);
    s.close();
  });

  it("ends the stream at the next heartbeat once the session is gone", async () => {
    const { state, authenticator } = revocable();
    const t = (current = await start({ authenticator }));
    const s = await t.open("alice");
    await s.next();
    state.revoked = true;
    const gone = t.nextEnd();
    t.beat();
    await gone;
    expect(await s.next()).toBe(": ping");
    await expect(s.next()).rejects.toThrow(/^stream ended$/);
  });

  it("fails closed: a re-check that throws ends the stream", async () => {
    const { state, authenticator } = revocable();
    state.fails = true;
    const t = (current = await start({ authenticator }));
    const s = await t.open("alice");
    await s.next();
    state.revoked = true;
    const gone = t.nextEnd();
    t.beat();
    await gone;
    await expect(s.next().then(() => s.next())).rejects.toThrow(/^stream ended$/);
  });
});
```

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { PG_URL } from "../db/testSupport";
import { ALICE, ALICE2, openSse } from "../documents/testHarness";
import { chatConversationId } from "./ids";
import { seedChat, startChatServer } from "./testHarness";

describe.skipIf(!PG_URL)("chat streams closeUser (needs DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL)", () => {
  it("ends one user's chat streams at once and frees their listeners, and no other user's", async () => {
    let ended: () => void = () => {};
    const h = await startChatServer({ stream: { onStreamEnd: () => ended() } });
    const nextEnd = () =>
      new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
        ended = resolve;
      });
    try {
      const [chatA, chatB] = [await seedChat(h, ALICE), await seedChat(h, ALICE2)];
      const open = async (chat: string, user: string) => {
        const s = await openSse(`${h.base}/api/chats/${chat}/stream`, { "x-test-user": user });
        await s.next(); // retry
        await s.next(); // status
        return s;
      };
      const [a, b] = [await open(chatA, "alice"), await open(chatB, "alice2")];
      const convA = chatConversationId(ALICE.userId, chatA);
      const convB = chatConversationId(ALICE2.userId, chatB);
      expect(h.wiring.hub.subscriberCount(convA)).toBe(1);

      const gone = nextEnd();
      h.streams.closeUser(ALICE.userId);
      await gone;
      await expect(a.next()).rejects.toThrow(/^stream ended$/);
      expect(h.wiring.hub.subscriberCount(convA)).toBe(0);
      expect(h.wiring.bus.subscriberCount(chatA)).toBe(0);
      // The other user's stream is still attached.
      expect(h.wiring.hub.subscriberCount(convB)).toBe(1);
      b.close();
    } finally {
      await h.close();
    }
  });

  it("is not a shutdown: the user's next stream opens", async () => {
    const h = await startChatServer();
    try {
      const chat = await seedChat(h, ALICE);
      h.streams.closeUser(ALICE.userId);
      const s = await openSse(`${h.base}/api/chats/${chat}/stream`, { "x-test-user": "alice" });
      expect(s.res.status).toBe(200);
      expect(await s.next()).toBe("retry: 3000");
      s.close();
    } finally {
      await h.close();
    }
  });
});
```

### Task 167: `forgetUser` for both read models, covering rows that have no scope

Implements findings E6 and E7. `IngestStatus.forgetUser` purges by `scope` only, and `ChatActivity` has no `forgetUser`. Neither reaches the rows whose key and scope are NULL: every chat and chat-title execution has a neutral row in `ingest-status`, and every ingest and title execution has one in `chat-activity` (Loom folds every execution for every model). They hold no status, but they are rows named by the user's execution id, and the erasure proof (D8) says no row names the user unless it is a tombstone.

`purgeFolded` has no prefix selector (exactly one of `executionId`, `key`, `scope`). So the model lists the user's remaining rows by execution-id prefix with a narrow read on the Loom pool, and tombstones each by `{ executionId }` through the service. Going through the service matters: it invalidates the fold engine's caches, and the tombstone keeps the watermark, so a late event cannot bring a row back. Before it purges, `forgetUser` drains the folds already scheduled, so a live fold in flight cannot write a row after its tombstone.

`forgetUser` keeps its existing contract (it throws synchronously on a bad id, and returns the number of rows newly tombstoned); the interface catalogue's `Promise<void>` is narrowed from that, not widened ("Deviations recorded" in the header). The lister is optional, so every existing construction keeps the scope-only behaviour. `compose` wires it in production.

**Files:**
- Create: `apps/server/app/status/forgetUser.ts`
- Modify: `apps/server/app/status/pgStores.ts` (`pgUserExecutions`)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/status/ingestStatus.ts`, `apps/server/app/chats/activity.ts` (`forgetUser`, option)
- Modify: `apps/server/app/compose.ts` (`StatusStores.userExecutions`, passed to both models), `apps/server/app/setup.ts`
- Test: `apps/server/app/status/forgetUser.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/status/forgetUser.pg.test.ts`, `apps/server/app/status/ingestStatus.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - Task 148: `ChatActivity`; M2: `IngestStatus.forgetUser` (scope), `pgStatusWriter`; Loom: `ReadModelService.purgeFolded/drain`.
  - Migration `0012`: `read_model_state (model_id, execution_id COLLATE "C", scope, erased)`; `docmind_loom` has SELECT and an `ingest_all` policy.
- Produces:
  - `type UserExecutionLister = (modelId: string, userId: string) => Promise<readonly string[]>` (the user's rows that are not yet tombstoned).
  - `forgetUserRows(service, modelId, userId, list?): Promise<number>` (throws `TypeError` synchronously for a non-uuid).
  - `pgUserExecutions(loomPool): UserExecutionLister`.
  - `IngestStatusOptions.userExecutions?` and `ChatActivityOptions.userExecutions?`; `ChatActivity.forgetUser(userId): Promise<number>`; `StatusStores.userExecutions?`.
