### Task 171: the item 34 proof, from sign-up to nothing left (spec §9 item 34, D8)

The milestone's one acceptance proof. It runs the production composition over real HTTP (`buildServer` and `bootIngest`, main's boot order; Loom on durable Postgres; the real chunks table and pgvector store; the real Loom conversation and agent), with `FakeAnthropic` and `startFakeOllama` as the only fakes, exactly as Task 155 did. Two users in one tenant each upload a document (to `ready`) and chat (reasoning, a tool step, a cited answer, an auto title). One of them calls `DELETE /api/me`. Right after the 202, before anything else, the proof checks what the request transaction alone did (the old email is in no table, the receipt reads `pending` or `done`). Then every claim of D8 is checked, against the database as the owner role sees it (nothing hides behind row-level security).

Split seam: **171a** is the stack extension, the support modules (exhaustive scan, log checks) and the erase-and-check test (including the solo-tenant case and the log hygiene check); **171b** is everything that happens next: the other user, the old credentials, a re-sign-up with the same email **before the eraser has run** (the receipt reading `pending`, then `done`), a re-sign-up after it, a re-run, two restarts with a forced read-model rebuild, a crash between steps with resume at boot, and an erasure that has to stop a running turn. Merge 171a first; 171b is stacked.

**Files (171a):**
- Modify: `apps/server/app/documents/ingestStack.ts` (the eraser is composed; option `wrapErasure`; `IngestStack.eraser`)
- Create: `apps/server/app/erasure/e2eSupport.ts` (`PASSWORD`, `SECRETS`, `Seeded`, `loginAs`, `addUserToTenant`, `seedUser`, `eraseMe`, `waitErased`), `apps/server/app/erasure/proof.ts` (`namingRows`, `assertErased`, `assertLogUnreadable`)
- Test: `apps/server/app/erasure/erasure.e2e.pg.test.ts`

**Files (171b):**
- Test: `apps/server/app/erasure/erasure.after.e2e.pg.test.ts` (neighbour, credentials, re-sign-up, re-run, restarts), `apps/server/app/erasure/erasure.resume.e2e.pg.test.ts` (crash between steps, running turn)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything in Tasks 164 to 170; Task 155's `startIngestStack`, `IngestStack` helpers (`signUpAndLogIn`, `upload`, `events`, `until`, `chatReq`, `openChat`, `claude`, `durable`, `keys`, `root`, `db`), `e2eSupport.ts` (`DOC`, `SENTENCE`, `SIG`, `collectUntil`, `isReply`, `scriptChat`), `eventLogProof.assertNoPlaintext`, `isPayloadShredded` from `@loom/event-runtime`, `hashPassword` from `@docmind/auth`.
- Produces:
  - `startIngestStack({ wrapErasure?: (deps: ErasureDeps) => ErasureDeps })`; the stack always composes the eraser with a 50 ms-capped backoff; `IngestStack.eraser: Eraser`.
  - `receiptState(st, receipt)`: `GET /api/erasure/<receipt>` with no cookie, asserting `Cache-Control: no-store`; returns `{ status, state? }`.
  - `seedUser(st, who, email): Promise<Seeded>` where `Seeded { email, cookie, userId, tenantId, documentId, passageId, chatId }`; `addUserToTenant(st, tenantId, email)` (a second user in an existing tenant); `eraseMe(st, cookie, password?)`; `waitErased(st, userId)`; `loginAs(st, email)`.
  - `namingRows(owner, { ids, emails? })`: the table.column pairs that hold any of the ids or emails, over **every** public base table column of type uuid, text, varchar or jsonb, found through `information_schema`. It skips the four tables that have their own narrower assertion (`erasure_requests`, `read_model_state`, `read_model_folds`, `data_keys`).
  - `assertErased(st, gone, { tenantGone, tombstones?, emailReused? })`: the whole of D8's "after" list for one user. `emailReused: true` leaves the email out of the scan, because a new account has taken the address.

- [ ] **Step 1 (171a): Write the failing test** (`erasure/erasure.e2e.pg.test.ts`).

```ts
import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { PG_URL } from "../db/testSupport";
import { type IngestStack, startIngestStack } from "../documents/ingestStack";
import { DeleteAccountResponse } from "@docmind/contracts";
import {
  SECRETS,
  addUserToTenant,
  eraseMe,
  loginAs,
  receiptState,
  seedUser,
  waitErased,
} from "./e2eSupport";
import { assertErased, namingRows } from "./proof";

const WAIT = 180_000;

describe.skipIf(!PG_URL)(
  "M5 acceptance: DELETE /api/me leaves nothing readable (needs DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL)",
  () => {
    let st: IngestStack;
    beforeAll(async () => {
      st = await startIngestStack({ chat: true });
    });
    afterAll(async () => {
      await st?.close();
    });

    it(
      "erases an account in a shared tenant: every row, blob, timer and key of the user is gone or unreadable, the colleague's data is not, and nothing sensitive is logged",
      async () => {
        const out = ["error", "log", "warn", "info"].map((m) =>
          vi.spyOn(console, m as "error").mockImplementation(() => {}),
        );
        try {
          const ada = await st.signUpAndLogIn("ada@example.com");
          const cam = await addUserToTenant(st, ada.tenantId, "cam@example.com");
          const adaData = await seedUser(st, ada, "ada@example.com");
          const camData = await seedUser(st, cam, "cam@example.com");
          const adaIds = [adaData.userId, adaData.documentId, adaData.chatId, adaData.passageId];
          const camIds = [camData.userId, camData.documentId, camData.chatId, camData.passageId];

          // The scan bites: before the erasure it finds the user in the tables we know hold them.
          const before = await namingRows(st.db.owner, { ids: adaIds, emails: [adaData.email] });
          expect(before).toEqual(
            expect.arrayContaining([
              "users.id",
              "users.email",
              "documents.owner_id",
              "documents.id",
              "chat_sessions.user_id",
              "chat_sessions.id",
            ]),
          );
          expect(before.length).toBeGreaterThan(8);
          const executions = (await st.durable.raw.eventStore.listExecutions?.()) ?? [];
          expect(executions.filter((id) => id.includes(adaData.userId)).length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(3); // conversation, ingest, title
          expect(await st.keys.get(adaData.userId)).not.toBeNull();

          // A wrong password erases nothing and keeps the session.
          const refused = await eraseMe(st, ada.cookie, "not the password");
          expect(refused.status).toBe(401);
          expect((await st.chatReq(ada.cookie, "GET", "/api/me")).status).toBe(200);
          expect(
            (await st.db.owner.query("SELECT count(*)::int AS n FROM erasure_requests")).rows[0].n,
          ).toBe(0);

          // The right one: 202 with a receipt, the cookie is cleared, the old cookie is already dead.
          const res = await eraseMe(st, ada.cookie);
          expect(res.status).toBe(202);
          const { erasure, receipt } = DeleteAccountResponse.parse(await res.json());
          expect(erasure).toBe("accepted");
          expect(res.headers.get("set-cookie")).toContain("Max-Age=0");
          expect((await st.chatReq(ada.cookie, "GET", "/api/me")).status).toBe(401);
          expect((await loginAs(st, "ada@example.com").catch((e: Error) => e.message))).toBe(
            "login failed 401",
          );

          // What the request transaction alone did, whatever the eraser has done by now: the old email is in no
          // table (users.email holds `erased:<id>`, or the row is already gone), and the receipt answers.
          expect(await namingRows(st.db.owner, { ids: [], emails: [adaData.email] })).toEqual([]);
          expect(["pending", "done"]).toContain((await receiptState(st, receipt)).state);
          // A stranger's receipt and a malformed one are the same 404.
          expect((await receiptState(st, DeleteAccountResponse.shape.receipt.parse("A".repeat(43)))).status).toBe(404);
          expect((await receiptState(st, "not-a-receipt")).status).toBe(404);

          await waitErased(st, adaData.userId);
          expect(await receiptState(st, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "done" });
          await assertErased(st, adaData, { tenantGone: false });

          // The colleague, in the same tenant, is untouched, and the scan finds them (it is not blind).
          const camRows = await namingRows(st.db.owner, { ids: camIds, emails: [camData.email] });
          expect(camRows).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(["users.id", "documents.owner_id", "chat_sessions.user_id"]));
          expect(await st.keys.get(camData.userId)).not.toBeNull();
          expect(existsSync(join(st.root, camData.tenantId, camData.userId))).toBe(true);

          // Nothing the eraser (or anything else) printed carries the user's id, email or text.
          const printed = JSON.stringify(out.flatMap((s) => s.mock.calls));
          for (const secret of [adaData.userId, adaData.email, adaData.documentId, adaData.chatId, receipt, ...SECRETS])
            expect(printed).not.toContain(secret);
        } finally {
          for (const s of out) s.mockRestore();
        }
      },
      WAIT,
    );

    it(
      "erases a user who is alone in their tenant: the personal tenant goes too",
      async () => {
        const dan = await st.signUpAndLogIn("dan@example.com");
        const danData = await seedUser(st, dan, "dan@example.com");
        const res = await eraseMe(st, dan.cookie);
        expect(res.status).toBe(202);
        const { receipt } = DeleteAccountResponse.parse(await res.json());
        await waitErased(st, danData.userId);
        expect(await receiptState(st, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "done" });
        await assertErased(st, danData, { tenantGone: true });
      },
      WAIT,
    );
  },
);
```

- [ ] **Step 4 (171a): Write the support module** (`erasure/e2eSupport.ts`).

```ts
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { hashPassword } from "@docmind/auth";
import { ChatSessionDto, ErasureStatusResponse, type UploadDocumentResponse } from "@docmind/contracts";
import { expect, vi } from "vitest";
import { DOC, SENTENCE, SIG, collectUntil, isReply, scriptChat } from "../chats/e2eSupport";
import type { IngestStack } from "../documents/ingestStack";

/** The password `startIngestStack().signUpAndLogIn` uses. */
export const PASSWORD = "correct horse battery";
export const QUESTION = "When is payment due?";
/** Distinctive texts (12+ characters) that must never be readable once the user is erased. */
export const SECRETS = [
  QUESTION,
  "Payment is due",
  "I should look for the payment terms",
  SENTENCE,
  SIG,
  "Payment due dates", // the auto title
  "Late payment accrues", // the rest of the passage
];

export interface Seeded {
  email: string;
  cookie: string;
  userId: string;
  tenantId: string;
  documentId: string;
  passageId: string;
  chatId: string;
}

/** A session for an existing account. Rejects with "login failed <status>" when the server refuses. */
export async function loginAs(st: IngestStack, email: string, password = PASSWORD): Promise<string> {
  await st.db.owner.query("DELETE FROM auth_throttle"); // the suite logs in more often than the throttle allows
  const res = await fetch(`${st.base}/api/auth/login`, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ email, password }),
  });
  if (res.status !== 200) throw new Error(`login failed ${res.status}`);
  return (res.headers.get("set-cookie") ?? "").split(";")[0] ?? "";
}

/**
 * A second user inside an existing tenant. Sign-up always makes a personal tenant, so the row goes in the way
 * the owner role would add it.
 */
export async function addUserToTenant(st: IngestStack, tenantId: string, email: string) {
  const userId = randomUUID();
  await st.db.owner.query(
    "INSERT INTO users (id, tenant_id, email, password_hash) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)",
    [userId, tenantId, email, await hashPassword(PASSWORD)],
  );
  return { cookie: await loginAs(st, email), userId, tenantId };
}

/**
 * Uploads a document and waits until it is ready, then holds one cited, reasoned chat turn and waits for its
 * auto title. Everything D8 lists as "before".
 */
export async function seedUser(
  st: IngestStack,
  who: { cookie: string; userId: string; tenantId: string },
  email: string,
): Promise<Seeded> {
  const claude = st.claude;
  if (!claude) throw new Error("the stack has no fake Claude");
  const events = await st.events(who.cookie);
  const up = await st.upload(who.cookie, "terms.txt", Buffer.from(DOC));
  expect(up.status).toBe(201);
  const documentId = ((await up.json()) as UploadDocumentResponse).document.id;
  const seen = await st.until(events, documentId, (s) => s === "ready" || s === "failed");
  events.close();
  expect(seen.at(-1)?.status).toBe("ready");
  const passageId = (
    await st.db.owner.query("SELECT id FROM chunks WHERE document_id = $1", [documentId])
  ).rows[0].id as string;
  scriptChat(claude, () => ({
    stream: {
      steps: [{ text: "Payment is due " }, { text: `within 30 days [[${passageId}]].` }, { stop: "end_turn" }],
    },
  }));
  const chat = ChatSessionDto.parse(
    await (await st.chatReq(who.cookie, "POST", "/api/chats", {})).json(),
  );
  const stream = await st.openChat(who.cookie, chat.id);
  try {
    const sent = await st.chatReq(who.cookie, "POST", `/api/chats/${chat.id}/messages`, {
      text: QUESTION,
    });
    expect(sent.status).toBe(202);
    await collectUntil(stream, isReply);
  } finally {
    stream.close();
  }
  await st.composed.activity.drain();
  await vi.waitFor(
    async () => {
      const one = ChatSessionDto.parse(
        await (await st.chatReq(who.cookie, "GET", `/api/chats/${chat.id}`)).json(),
      );
      expect(one.title).toBe("Payment due dates");
    },
    { timeout: 20_000 }, // the title flow is a durable execution with no hook to await
  );
  return { email, ...who, documentId, passageId, chatId: chat.id };
}

export const eraseMe = (st: IngestStack, cookie: string, password = PASSWORD) =>
  fetch(`${st.base}/api/me`, {
    method: "DELETE",
    headers: { cookie, "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ password }),
  });

/**
 * The state a receipt reads, as an anonymous caller (no cookie): `{ status: 200, state }`, or `{ status }` for a 404.
 * Every answer must be `no-store`.
 */
export async function receiptState(st: IngestStack, receipt: string): Promise<{ status: number; state?: string }> {
  const res = await fetch(`${st.base}/api/erasure/${receipt}`);
  expect(res.headers.get("cache-control")).toBe("no-store");
  if (res.status !== 200) return { status: res.status };
  return { status: 200, state: ErasureStatusResponse.parse(await res.json()).state };
}

/** Resolves when the user's request is `done`. */
export const waitErased = (st: IngestStack, userId: string, timeout = 90_000) =>
  vi.waitFor(
    async () => {
      const { rows } = await st.db.owner.query(
        "SELECT state FROM erasure_requests WHERE user_id = $1",
        [userId],
      );
      expect(rows[0]?.state).toBe("done");
    },
    { timeout, interval: 100 },
  );
```

- [ ] **Step 5 (171a): Write the proof module** (`erasure/proof.ts`). `namingRows` is the exhaustive part: the column list comes from the catalog, so a table added by a later migration is scanned without anyone remembering to add it. The other checks are the narrower ones D8 lists.

```ts
import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { isPayloadShredded } from "@loom/event-runtime";
import type { Pool } from "pg";
import { expect } from "vitest";
import { assertNoPlaintext } from "../chats/eventLogProof";
import type { IngestStack } from "../documents/ingestStack";
import { SECRETS, type Seeded } from "./e2eSupport";
import { STEP_ORDER } from "./eraser";

const quote = (name: string) => `"${name.replaceAll('"', '""')}"`;

/** Tables with an assertion of their own in `assertErased`: they legitimately keep ids (or rows) after an erasure. */
const OWN_ASSERTION = new Set(["erasure_requests", "read_model_state", "read_model_folds", "data_keys"]);

/**
 * Every `table.column` (public base tables, columns of type uuid, text, varchar or jsonb) that holds any of `ids`
 * or `emails`: uuid columns by equality, text columns by equality or by an execution-id prefix (`u_<id>...`), jsonb
 * columns by substring (ids and emails). Read as the owner role, so row-level security hides nothing. With
 * `ids: []` it is a pure email scan.
 */
export async function namingRows(
  owner: Pool,
  who: { ids: readonly string[]; emails?: readonly string[] },
): Promise<string[]> {
  const { rows: columns } = await owner.query(
    `SELECT c.table_name AS t, c.column_name AS c, c.data_type AS d
       FROM information_schema.columns c
       JOIN information_schema.tables x
         ON x.table_schema = c.table_schema AND x.table_name = c.table_name
      WHERE c.table_schema = 'public' AND x.table_type = 'BASE TABLE'
        AND c.data_type IN ('uuid', 'text', 'character varying', 'jsonb')
      ORDER BY c.table_name, c.column_name`,
  );
  const ids = [...who.ids];
  const texts = [...ids, ...(who.emails ?? [])];
  const patterns = texts.map((s) => `%${s}%`); // jsonb is searched for the ids and the emails alike
  const hits: string[] = [];
  for (const { t, c, d } of columns as { t: string; c: string; d: string }[]) {
    if (OWN_ASSERTION.has(t)) continue;
    const col = quote(c);
    const [where, args] =
      d === "uuid"
        ? [`${col} = ANY($1::uuid[])`, [ids]]
        : d === "jsonb"
          ? [`${col}::text LIKE ANY($1::text[])`, [patterns]]
          : [
              `${col} = ANY($1::text[]) OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM unnest($2::text[]) AS p WHERE starts_with(${col}, 'u_' || p))`,
              [texts, ids],
            ];
    const found = await owner.query(`SELECT 1 FROM public.${quote(t)} WHERE ${where} LIMIT 1`, args);
    if (found.rowCount) hits.push(`${t}.${c}`);
  }
  return hits;
}

/**
 * The user's Loom executions: the raw log holds no readable payload (no secret in the clear, no envelope that
 * still opens), every execution that has a sealed payload carries the shred tombstone, and reading through
 * the decrypting store refuses with a shredded error, never with content.
 */
export async function assertLogUnreadable(st: IngestStack, userId: string): Promise<void> {
  const ids = ((await st.durable.raw.eventStore.listExecutions?.()) ?? []).filter((id) =>
    id.includes(userId),
  );
  expect(ids.length).toBeGreaterThan(0); // an empty list would pass everything below
  const raw = (await Promise.all(ids.map((id) => st.durable.raw.eventStore.read(id)))).flat();
  assertNoPlaintext(raw, SECRETS);
  let refused = 0;
  for (const id of ids) {
    const events = raw.filter((e) => e.executionId === id);
    if (JSON.stringify(events).includes("__loomEnc"))
      expect(events.some((e) => e.eventType === "PAYLOAD_SHREDDED")).toBe(true);
    try {
      const opened = await st.durable.sealed.eventStore.read(id);
      expect(JSON.stringify(opened)).not.toContain("__loomEnc"); // a read that works shows no envelope either
      assertNoPlaintext(opened, SECRETS);
    } catch (err) {
      expect(isPayloadShredded(err)).toBe(true);
      refused++;
    }
  }
  expect(refused).toBeGreaterThan(0); // the sealed conversation and ingest logs did refuse
  // The sealed snapshot of the conversation cannot be opened either.
  const conversation = ids.find((id) => id.includes(".c-") && !id.includes("~"));
  if (conversation) await expect(st.durable.sealed.snapshotStore.loadLatest(conversation)).rejects.toThrow();
}

/** Every claim of D8's "after" list, for one erased user. */
export async function assertErased(
  st: IngestStack,
  gone: Seeded,
  opts: { tenantGone: boolean; tombstones?: boolean; emailReused?: boolean },
): Promise<void> {
  const { db, keys } = st;
  const ids = [gone.userId, gone.documentId, gone.chatId, gone.passageId];

  // 1. No row of the user or of what they owned, in any column that could name it. (When a new account has taken
  //    the same address, the email is left out: the ids still prove nothing of the old user is left.)
  expect(await namingRows(db.owner, { ids, emails: opts.emailReused ? [] : [gone.email] })).toEqual([]);

  // 2. The read models: only tombstones (state NULL, erased), and the tombstones are really there.
  const live = await db.owner.query(
    `SELECT model_id FROM read_model_state
      WHERE (scope = $1 OR starts_with(execution_id, $2) OR app_key = ANY($3::text[]))
        AND (state IS NOT NULL OR NOT erased)`,
    [gone.userId, `u_${gone.userId}`, [gone.documentId, gone.chatId]],
  );
  expect(live.rows).toEqual([]);
  if (opts.tombstones !== false) {
    const tomb = await db.owner.query(
      "SELECT DISTINCT model_id FROM read_model_state WHERE starts_with(execution_id, $1) AND erased",
      [`u_${gone.userId}`],
    );
    expect(tomb.rows.map((r) => r.model_id).sort()).toEqual(["chat-activity", "ingest-status"]);
  }

  // 3. The key: destroyed, remembered as destroyed, and the tombstone row holds no key material.
  expect(await keys.wasDestroyed(gone.userId)).toBe(true);
  expect(await keys.get(gone.userId)).toBeNull();
  const key = await db.owner.query(
    "SELECT wrapped_dek IS NULL AS no_key, destroyed_at IS NOT NULL AS gone FROM data_keys WHERE subject_id = $1",
    [gone.userId],
  );
  expect(key.rows).toEqual([{ no_key: true, gone: true }]);

  // 4. No blob directory.
  expect(existsSync(join(st.root, gone.tenantId, gone.userId))).toBe(false);

  // 5. No timers, no leases.
  for (const table of ["timers", "leases"]) {
    const left = await st.durable.pool.query(
      `SELECT count(*)::int AS n FROM loom.${table} WHERE starts_with(execution_id, $1)`,
      [`u_${gone.userId}`],
    );
    expect(left.rows[0].n).toBe(0);
  }

  // 6. The log: nothing readable.
  await assertLogUnreadable(st, gone.userId);

  // 7. The audit row: one, done, every step recorded in order, ids and times only.
  const audit = await db.owner.query(
    "SELECT state, steps, completed_at IS NOT NULL AS completed FROM erasure_requests WHERE user_id = $1",
    [gone.userId],
  );
  expect(audit.rows).toEqual([{ state: "done", steps: [...STEP_ORDER], completed: true }]);

  // 8. The tenant: gone with its last user, kept while anyone is left.
  const tenant = await db.owner.query("SELECT count(*)::int AS n FROM tenants WHERE id = $1", [gone.tenantId]);
  expect(tenant.rows[0].n).toBe(opts.tenantGone ? 0 : 1);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 8 (171b): Write the failing tests for what happens next** (`erasure/erasure.after.e2e.pg.test.ts`). One stack, one story: ada and cam share a tenant; ada erases; then everything D8 says about the world afterwards.

```ts
import { rmSync } from "node:fs";
import {
  DeleteAccountResponse,
  ListChatsResponse,
  ListDocumentsResponse,
  ListMessagesResponse,
} from "@docmind/contracts";
import { afterAll, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { collectUntil, isReply, scriptChat } from "../chats/e2eSupport";
import { PG_URL } from "../db/testSupport";
import { type IngestStack, startIngestStack } from "../documents/ingestStack";
import { type Seeded, addUserToTenant, eraseMe, loginAs, receiptState, seedUser, waitErased } from "./e2eSupport";
import { assertErased, namingRows } from "./proof";

const WAIT = 300_000;

describe.skipIf(!PG_URL)("M5 acceptance: after the erasure (needs DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL)", () => {
  const stacks: IngestStack[] = [];
  const track = (s: IngestStack) => (stacks.push(s), s);
  afterAll(async () => {
    const first = stacks[0];
    for (const s of stacks.splice(0)) await s.close({ keepDb: true });
    if (first) {
      await first.db.drop();
      rmSync(first.root, { recursive: true, force: true });
    }
  });

  /** What a signed-in user sees: documents, chats, the messages of their chat, and the reasoning of its reply. */
  async function observe(st: IngestStack, who: Seeded) {
    const docs = ListDocumentsResponse.parse(await (await st.chatReq(who.cookie, "GET", "/api/documents")).json());
    const chats = ListChatsResponse.parse(await (await st.chatReq(who.cookie, "GET", "/api/chats")).json());
    const history = ListMessagesResponse.parse(
      await (await st.chatReq(who.cookie, "GET", `/api/chats/${who.chatId}/messages`)).json(),
    );
    const reply = history.items.find((m) => m.role === "assistant");
    const reasoning = await (
      await st.chatReq(who.cookie, "GET", `/api/chats/${who.chatId}/reasoning?cursor=${reply?.cursor}`)
    ).json();
    return {
      documents: docs.items.map((d) => [d.id, d.status]),
      chats: chats.items.map((c) => [c.id, c.title, c.messageCount]),
      history: history.items,
      reasoning,
    };
  }
  /** The erased user's footprint in the log and the audit row: a re-run must not change it. */
  async function footprint(st: IngestStack, who: Seeded) {
    const ids = ((await st.durable.raw.eventStore.listExecutions?.()) ?? []).filter((id) => id.includes(who.userId));
    const counts = await Promise.all(ids.map(async (id) => (await st.durable.raw.eventStore.read(id)).length));
    const audit = await st.db.owner.query(
      "SELECT state, steps, updated_at, completed_at FROM erasure_requests WHERE user_id = $1",
      [who.userId],
    );
    return { ids: ids.sort(), counts, audit: audit.rows };
  }

  it(
    "the neighbour keeps working, the old credentials are dead, a re-run changes nothing, sign-up starts fresh, and the server restarts twice (once with a forced read-model rebuild)",
    async () => {
      const errors = vi.spyOn(console, "error").mockImplementation(() => {});
      const first = track(await startIngestStack({ chat: true }));
      const claude = first.claude;
      if (!claude) throw new Error("no fake Claude");
      const adaLogin = await first.signUpAndLogIn("ada@example.com");
      const camLogin = await addUserToTenant(first, adaLogin.tenantId, "cam@example.com");
      const ada = await seedUser(first, adaLogin, "ada@example.com");
      const cam = await seedUser(first, camLogin, "cam@example.com");
      const camBefore = await observe(first, cam);
      expect(camBefore.documents).toEqual([[cam.documentId, "ready"]]);
      expect(camBefore.chats).toEqual([[cam.chatId, "Payment due dates", 2]]);

      const erased = await eraseMe(first, ada.cookie);
      expect(erased.status).toBe(202);
      const { receipt } = DeleteAccountResponse.parse(await erased.json());
      await waitErased(first, ada.userId);
      expect(await receiptState(first, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "done" });
      await assertErased(first, ada, { tenantGone: false });

      // 1. the colleague is untouched, and can keep chatting (the conversation under the same tenant still runs)
      expect(await observe(first, cam)).toEqual(camBefore);
      scriptChat(claude, () => ({ stream: { steps: [{ text: "Two percent per month." }, { stop: "end_turn" }] } }));
      const stream = await first.openChat(cam.cookie, cam.chatId);
      try {
        const sent = await first.chatReq(cam.cookie, "POST", `/api/chats/${cam.chatId}/messages`, {
          text: "And what about late payment?",
        });
        expect(sent.status).toBe(202);
        await collectUntil(stream, (f) => isReply(f) && (f.data as { text: string }).text === "Two percent per month.");
      } finally {
        stream.close();
      }
      const camAfterTurn = await observe(first, cam);
      expect(camAfterTurn.history.map((m) => m.role)).toEqual(["user", "assistant", "user", "assistant"]);

      // 2. the old credentials fail, with the answer of a wrong password (no account has that email: it was
      //    replaced at the request); the old cookie is dead
      await expect(loginAs(first, "ada@example.com")).rejects.toThrow("login failed 401");
      expect((await first.chatReq(ada.cookie, "GET", "/api/me")).status).toBe(401);

      // 3. re-running is a no-op: the same footprint, the same audit row, no model call
      const footprintBefore = await footprint(first, ada);
      const modelCalls = claude.requests.length;
      await first.eraser.run(ada.userId);
      await first.eraser.resumeAll();
      await first.eraser.idle();
      expect(await footprint(first, ada)).toEqual(footprintBefore);
      expect(claude.requests.length).toBe(modelCalls);
      await assertErased(first, ada, { tenantGone: false });

      // 4. the same email signs up again (after the erasure; the next test does it before the eraser has run),
      //    as a different, empty account
      const again = await first.signUpAndLogIn("ada@example.com");
      expect(again.userId).not.toBe(ada.userId);
      expect(again.tenantId).not.toBe(ada.tenantId);
      const docs = ListDocumentsResponse.parse(await (await first.chatReq(again.cookie, "GET", "/api/documents")).json());
      const chats = ListChatsResponse.parse(await (await first.chatReq(again.cookie, "GET", "/api/chats")).json());
      expect([docs.items, chats.items]).toEqual([[], []]);
      expect(await namingRows(first.db.owner, { ids: [ada.userId, ada.documentId, ada.chatId, ada.passageId] })).toEqual([]);
      expect(await receiptState(first, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "done" }); // a done receipt keeps answering

      // 5. restart: catch-up over a log that holds a shredded user boots, and nothing changes
      await first.close({ keepDb: true });
      stacks.splice(stacks.indexOf(first), 1);
      const resume = { db: first.db, kek: first.kek, root: first.root };
      const second = track(await startIngestStack({ chat: true, resume }));
      expect(await observe(second, cam)).toEqual(camAfterTurn);
      await assertErased(second, ada, { tenantGone: false, emailReused: true }); // ada's address belongs to the new account now
      expect(await receiptState(second, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "done" }); // ... and the receipt survives a restart

      // 6. restart again, after throwing the read models away: every model refolds from the start over the
      //    shredded logs. The restart goes through the real compose boot path (`startIngestStack` composes
      //    `buildServer` and `bootIngest`), so after the models catch up `afterListen` calls `Eraser.resumeAll`,
      //    which re-runs `forgetUser` for every done erasure and re-tombstones whatever the refold re-created for
      //    the erased user. The strict `live` query of `assertErased` therefore stays as it is: no row of the
      //    erased user is live. The test awaits one more `resumeAll` (idempotent) only to make the wait deterministic.
      await second.close({ keepDb: true });
      stacks.splice(stacks.indexOf(second), 1);
      await second.db.owner.query("DELETE FROM read_model_state");
      await second.db.owner.query("DELETE FROM read_model_folds");
      const third = track(await startIngestStack({ chat: true, resume }));
      await third.eraser.resumeAll();
      expect(await observe(third, cam)).toEqual(camAfterTurn);
      await assertErased(third, ada, { tenantGone: false, tombstones: false, emailReused: true });
      errors.mockRestore();
    },
    WAIT,
  );

  it(
    "a re-sign-up with the same email works the moment the 202 lands, before the eraser has run: the old email is in no table, and the receipt reads pending, then done",
    async () => {
      vi.spyOn(console, "error").mockImplementation(() => {});
      // The eraser is held at its lock until released: nothing of the erasure runs in between, so what the
      // assertions see is what the request transaction alone did.
      let release: () => void = () => {};
      const gate = new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
        release = resolve;
      });
      const st = track(
        await startIngestStack({
          chat: true,
          wrapErasure: (deps) => ({ ...deps, lock: async (userId) => (await gate, deps.lock(userId)) }),
        }),
      );
      const login = await st.signUpAndLogIn("gus@example.com");
      const gus = await seedUser(st, login, "gus@example.com");
      let receipt = "";
      let again: Awaited<ReturnType<IngestStack["signUpAndLogIn"]>>;
      try {
        const res = await eraseMe(st, gus.cookie);
        expect(res.status).toBe(202);
        receipt = DeleteAccountResponse.parse(await res.json()).receipt;

        // 1. nothing has been erased yet: the request is pending with no step done, and the receipt says so
        const audit = await st.db.owner.query("SELECT state, steps FROM erasure_requests WHERE user_id = $1", [gus.userId]);
        expect(audit.rows).toEqual([{ state: "pending", steps: [] }]);
        expect(await receiptState(st, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "pending" });
        expect(await st.keys.get(gus.userId)).not.toBeNull(); // the key is alive: the shred has not run

        // 2. and yet the old email is in no table: the row still exists, under the placeholder
        expect(await namingRows(st.db.owner, { ids: [], emails: [gus.email] })).toEqual([]);
        expect((await st.db.owner.query("SELECT email FROM users WHERE id = $1", [gus.userId])).rows).toEqual([
          { email: `erased:${gus.userId}` },
        ]);

        // 3. the old credentials are dead
        await expect(loginAs(st, gus.email)).rejects.toThrow("login failed 401");

        // 4. the same email signs up at once, as a new user in a new tenant, who sees nothing of the old one
        again = await st.signUpAndLogIn("gus@example.com");
        expect(again.userId).not.toBe(gus.userId);
        expect(again.tenantId).not.toBe(gus.tenantId);
        const docs = ListDocumentsResponse.parse(await (await st.chatReq(again.cookie, "GET", "/api/documents")).json());
        const chats = ListChatsResponse.parse(await (await st.chatReq(again.cookie, "GET", "/api/chats")).json());
        expect([docs.items, chats.items]).toEqual([[], []]);
        expect(await receiptState(st, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "pending" }); // still the old erasure's
      } finally {
        release();
      }

      // 5. the eraser runs: the receipt reads done, nothing of the old user is left, the new account is untouched
      await waitErased(st, gus.userId);
      expect(await receiptState(st, receipt)).toEqual({ status: 200, state: "done" });
      await assertErased(st, gus, { tenantGone: true, emailReused: true });
      expect((await st.chatReq(again.cookie, "GET", "/api/me")).status).toBe(200);
      expect((await st.db.owner.query("SELECT email FROM users WHERE id = $1", [again.userId])).rows).toEqual([
        { email: "gus@example.com" },
      ]);
    },
    WAIT,
  );
});
```

- [ ] **Step 9 (171b): Run it.** `export DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL=… && pnpm -C apps/server vitest run app/erasure/erasure.after.e2e.pg.test.ts`. Expected: PASS (2 tests), without any change to production code: 171a's support is all it needs, and Tasks 164 to 170 are what is under test. If step 6 fails with a poison-event or `PayloadShreddedError` message at boot, Task 165's tolerant source or a handler's skip is missing for that model: that is the E5 hazard, and the failure is the proof doing its job.

  The first `afterAll` closes every still-running stack and drops the one database (the three stacks of the story test share it; the gated re-sign-up test has its own stack, which `track` closes too) and the blob root once. If the gated test hangs in its `finally`/`waitErased`, the gate was never released: the `lock` wrapper holds the eraser, and only `release()` lets it run.

- [ ] **Step 10 (171b): Write the crash-resume and running-turn tests** (`erasure/erasure.resume.e2e.pg.test.ts`).

```ts
import { rmSync } from "node:fs";
import { afterAll, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { scriptChat } from "../chats/e2eSupport";
import { PG_URL } from "../db/testSupport";
import { type IngestStack, startIngestStack } from "../documents/ingestStack";
import { loginAs, eraseMe, seedUser, waitErased } from "./e2eSupport";
import type { ErasureDeps } from "./eraser";
import { assertErased } from "./proof";

const WAIT = 300_000;

describe.skipIf(!PG_URL)("M5 acceptance: an interrupted or deferred erasure completes (needs DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL)", () => {
  const stacks: IngestStack[] = [];
  const dbs = new Set<IngestStack["db"]>();
  const roots = new Set<string>();
  const track = (s: IngestStack) => (stacks.push(s), dbs.add(s.db), roots.add(s.root), s);
  afterAll(async () => {
    for (const s of stacks.splice(0)) await s.close({ keepDb: true });
    for (const db of dbs) await db.drop();
    for (const root of roots) rmSync(root, { recursive: true, force: true });
  });

  it(
    "a process that dies between the shred and the rows resumes at boot: the half-erased account is dead, then it is gone",
    async () => {
      vi.spyOn(console, "error").mockImplementation(() => {}); // the failing step logs its class on every retry
      // Everything up to and including the shred runs; the `rows` step then fails until the process is "killed".
      const wrap = (deps: ErasureDeps): ErasureDeps => ({
        ...deps,
        rows: async () => {
          throw new Error("simulated crash before the rows step");
        },
      });
      const first = track(await startIngestStack({ chat: true, wrapErasure: wrap }));
      const login = await first.signUpAndLogIn("eve@example.com");
      const eve = await seedUser(first, login, "eve@example.com");
      expect((await eraseMe(first, eve.cookie)).status).toBe(202);

      await vi.waitFor(
        async () => {
          const { rows } = await first.db.owner.query("SELECT steps FROM erasure_requests WHERE user_id = $1", [eve.userId]);
          expect(rows[0]?.steps).toEqual(["stop", "shred", "readModels"]);
        },
        { timeout: 90_000, interval: 100 },
      );
      // The half-erased state: the key is gone, the sealed log refuses, the row is still there and marked.
      expect(await first.keys.wasDestroyed(eve.userId)).toBe(true);
      const row = await first.db.owner.query("SELECT erasing_at IS NOT NULL AS erasing FROM users WHERE id = $1", [eve.userId]);
      expect(row.rows).toEqual([{ erasing: true }]);
      await expect(loginAs(first, "eve@example.com")).rejects.toThrow("login failed 401"); // erasing: refused
      expect((await first.db.owner.query("SELECT state FROM erasure_requests WHERE user_id = $1", [eve.userId])).rows[0].state).toBe("pending");

      // "Kill" the process: the request is durable, the eraser stops where it is.
      await first.close({ keepDb: true });
      stacks.splice(stacks.indexOf(first), 1);

      // The next boot finds the pending request and finishes it, with no help from any request.
      const second = track(await startIngestStack({ chat: true, resume: { db: first.db, kek: first.kek, root: first.root } }));
      await waitErased(second, eve.userId);
      await assertErased(second, eve, { tenantGone: true });
    },
    WAIT,
  );

  it(
    "an erasure that finds a chat turn running stops it, waits for the conversation to park, and completes",
    async () => {
      vi.spyOn(console, "error").mockImplementation(() => {});
      const st = track(await startIngestStack({ chat: true }));
      const claude = st.claude;
      if (!claude) throw new Error("no fake Claude");
      const login = await st.signUpAndLogIn("fay@example.com");
      const fay = await seedUser(st, login, "fay@example.com");

      // A second question whose answer stops mid-stream and waits: the turn is running when the erasure begins.
      scriptChat(claude, () => ({
        stream: { steps: [{ text: "Due " }, { hold: true }, { text: "in 30 days." }, { stop: "end_turn" }] },
      }));
      const sent = await st.chatReq(fay.cookie, "POST", `/api/chats/${fay.chatId}/messages`, { text: "And the interest?" });
      expect(sent.status).toBe(202);
      const gate = await claude.streamHold(); // the model call is under way
      try {
        expect((await eraseMe(st, fay.cookie)).status).toBe(202);
        await waitErased(st, fay.userId);
      } finally {
        gate.release();
      }
      await assertErased(st, fay, { tenantGone: true });
    },
    WAIT,
  );
});
```

### Task 172: docs: spec §4.4 as built, the trade-offs, the four cross-tenant paths (six definer functions), README operations and status (spec §9 item 48 in part)

Brings the spec and the README in line with what M5 built. No code. The M7 runbook (item 48) still owns the full backup and restore drill; this task writes down the rules that exist today: what an erasure does and in which order, what stays by design, and the 30-day backup retention.

**Files:**
- Modify: `docs/specs/2026-10-18-docmind-design.md` (§4.3 point 5; §4.4 flow, table row, trade-offs 7 to 9; the roadmap line for item 34)
- Modify: `README.org` (intro, Status table, Architecture item 6 and the security model, Operations: Erasure and Backups; the last sentence before "Deployment notes")
- Modify: `.env.example` only if a new setting exists (none does; Step 5 checks)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 164 to 171 as built.
- Produces: the written contract of erasure. Nothing else depends on it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Spec §4.3.** After point 4 (Blobs), add a fifth point that lists the cross-tenant paths, because a reviewer needs one place to see everything that is allowed to cross a tenant:

```markdown
5. **Cross-tenant paths: exactly four.** Everything else runs inside `withTenant`.
   1. Loom's recovery scan (it resumes executions of every tenant after a restart).
   2. The read-model writer (`docmind_loom`): it folds and tombstones rows of every tenant, and the policy lets only that role do it.
   3. The upload sweep's two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions (stale uploads and orphaned status rows, ids only).
   4. Erasure: `docmind_erasure_pending(p_state)` (lists the durable requests that are `pending` or `done`), `docmind_erasure_mark` (the durable request table, ids and step names only), `docmind_erasure_status(p_receipt_hash)` (takes the hash of an erasure receipt and returns only a state, `pending`, `done` or NULL, for the unauthenticated receipt route) and `docmind_delete_empty_tenant` (deletes a tenant only when no user is left). Each pins `search_path`, is revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `docmind_app` alone. Counted by name, paths 3 and 4 are six definer functions.
   The login, session and sign-up functions of 0005 are a separate, older class: they run before a tenant is known, and return one row.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Spec §4.4, the flow.** Replace the "Erasure flow" bullet and its four numbered steps with the flow as built:

```markdown
- **Erasure flow.** `DELETE /api/me` (body `{password}`) is behind the session and the origin guard, and
  checks the password with the login hasher under the login throttle (a wrong password is 401
  `invalid_credentials`). In one transaction it inserts a durable `erasure_requests` row (holding the
  sha256 of a receipt token, never the token), deletes every session of the user, sets `users.erasing_at`
  and replaces `users.email` with `erased:<user id>`. It then answers **202**
  `{erasure: "accepted", receipt}` (the receipt is 32 random bytes, base64url, shown once), clears the
  cookie and starts the `Eraser` in the background. From then on login cannot match the old email (and
  `erasing_at` refuses the row a second time), the old email is in no table, and **signing up with the same
  email works at once**, as a new account, even while the erasure is pending. `GET /api/erasure/:receipt`
  (no session, throttled per IP, `Cache-Control: no-store`) answers `{state: "pending" | "done"}`, or 404 for
  an unknown or malformed receipt. The eraser runs these steps in order; each is
  idempotent and recorded on the request, so a crash resumes at the first unrecorded step (every pending
  request is resumed at boot, after recovery starts):
  1. `stop`: close the user's event streams, cancel every ingest generation of each document, stop and
     close each chat, cancel each chat-title execution.
  2. `shred`: `app.eraseSubject(userId, undefined, {parked: "shred"})` (no tenant: DocMind has one subject partition). Loom cancels the user's parked
     conversations, appends the shred tombstones and **destroys the key** (`shredSubject` calls
     `KeyStore.destroy` itself), so the spec's old step 4 now happens here: the user's sealed columns
     and blobs are unreadable from this point. A conversation that is mid-turn still defers the shred; the
     eraser stops it again and retries with a backoff of 1 s, 2 s, 4 s, ... at most 60 s, without a limit.
  3. `readModels`: tombstone the user's `ingest-status` and `chat-activity` rows (by scope and by
     `u_<user>` execution-id prefix).
  4. `rows`: delete the `users` row (the foreign keys cascade to sessions, documents, chunks, parsed text,
     links, entities, chats, turn items and reasoning texts), delete the user's leftover Loom timers and
     leases, and delete the personal tenant if no user is left in it. (The login and sign-up throttle
     counters of the email were cleared by the route, before the email was replaced.)
  5. `blobs`: delete the user's blob directory.
  6. `key`: destroy the key again (idempotent, and it covers a user the shred never saw), assert it is
     destroyed, and sweep the blobs once more (an upload that was already streaming when the erase began).
  7. `done`.

  One process runs one erasure per user (a map, plus a Postgres advisory lock `erasure:<user>`). The
  eraser logs the step name, the error class and the attempt, and never an id, email, receipt or content.
  A step that has failed 10 times (`ERASURE_STUCK_AFTER`) also logs one line,
  `erasure stuck <step> <ErrorClass> attempts=<n>`, repeated every 10 further attempts.
  Loom's subject index is rebuilt at boot from the first event of every `u_<uuid>` execution, and the
  read models read the log through a tolerant source and skip shredded events, so a restart, a version
  bump or a rebuild never trips over an erased user.
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Spec §4.4, the table and the trade-offs.** In the table, change the `*_enc` row's last cell to `Unreadable at step 2; rows hard-deleted at step 4`, and the Blobs row's to `Ciphertext only; the files are deleted at step 5`. Append three trade-offs after item 6:

```markdown
  7. What stays after an erasure, by design (decision D7), none of it readable content: in Loom's log the
     execution ids (`u_<user>:...`, a plaintext UUID and a fixed name), event types, timestamps, the shred
     tombstones and the `EXECUTION_CANCELLED` reason `erased`; the payloads are ciphertext under a destroyed
     key. `data_keys` keeps a tombstone row (the subject id and the time of destruction, no key material) so
     a destroyed subject can never be given a new key. `read_model_state` keeps tombstoned rows (ids, no
     state), which is what stops a later catch-up from resurrecting a row. `erasure_requests` keeps the
     audit row: user and tenant id, the sha256 of the receipt token, step names and times. Loom's events are not deleted outright, because its
     own consistency (continuations, tombstone sequence numbers) depends on the log being whole.
  8. Backups made before an erasure hold the user's rows, embeddings and ciphertext until they expire, and
     a restored key-store backup holds the key. Backups are kept for at most 30 days (the operations
     section of the README), and after any restore the erasure requests are re-run from `erasure_requests`
     (`pending` ones resume by themselves; for a `done` one, insert it again as pending).
  9. The erasure is asynchronous (202). Between the 202 and `done` the account is already unusable, and its
     email is already free (it is replaced in the request transaction), but some of its other rows may still
     exist for as long as a running chat turn keeps the shred waiting. The receipt is the only way to see
     where it stands; anyone who holds the token can read `pending` or `done` and nothing else.
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Spec §9.** Item 34 gains `*(done, M5, Tasks 164 to 172)*` the way items 26 and 33 are marked. Run `grep -n "^34\." docs/specs/2026-10-18-docmind-design.md` first and edit that line only.

- [ ] **Step 5: `.env.example`.** M5 adds no setting (the eraser has no tunables: the backoff, 1 s to 60 s, and the stuck threshold, `ERASURE_STUCK_AFTER = 10`, are fixed). Run `git diff --stat -- .env.example apps/server/app/config.ts apps/server/app/chats/settings.ts` and `grep -rn "process.env\\." apps/server/app/erasure` (expected: no output). No change to `.env.example`, and none to the README's variable table.

- [ ] **Step 6: README, the top and the Status table.** Replace the sentence "Document storage, ingestion, linked retrieval and chat exist today (see Status); the web UI is planned." with "Document storage, ingestion, linked retrieval, chat and account erasure exist today (see Status); the web UI is planned." In the table: M3 `complete` becomes `shipped`, M4 `complete` becomes `shipped`, M5 `planned` becomes `shipped` and its scope cell becomes `Erasure: =DELETE /api/me=, crypto-shredding end to end`. Keep the column padding that Org needs (re-align the `|` bars with `C-c C-c` in Emacs, or by hand).

- [ ] **Step 7: README, architecture and security.** Add after Architecture item 5:

```org
6. =DELETE /api/me= (body =password=) erases the account: it records a durable request, logs the user out
   everywhere, answers 202, and a background =Eraser= stops the user's work, destroys the user's key (which
   makes every event, snapshot, sealed column and blob unreadable), tombstones the read models, deletes the
   rows, deletes the blobs and finishes. An interrupted erasure resumes at boot. See Operations.
```

  and replace the security model bullet "Destroying a user's key makes their events, snapshots and blobs unreadable (crypto-shredding)." with "Destroying a user's key makes their events, snapshots, sealed columns and blobs unreadable (crypto-shredding); =DELETE /api/me= does that and then deletes their rows and files."

- [ ] **Step 8: README, Operations.** Insert before the "Cost:" bullet:

```org
- Erasure: =DELETE /api/me= with ={"password": "..."}= answers 202 and erases in the background.
  Watch it with =SELECT state, steps FROM erasure_requests= (as the owner role): =steps= lists the steps
  done, in order (=stop=, =shred=, =readModels=, =rows=, =blobs=, =key=, =done=), and =state= is =done= at
  the end. A step that fails is retried with a backoff of 1 s to 60 s, and the log shows only the step name,
  the error class and the attempt. A shred that waits on a running chat turn logs =ErasureParkedError=
  until the turn ends (a stop request has been sent; this is normally seconds). Erasure resumes by itself
  at the next boot; to re-run one by hand, start the server. Re-running a finished one is harmless. What
  stays afterwards is listed in the spec (§4.4, trade-off 7): ids, event types, tombstones and the audit row,
  none of it content.
  The 202 body carries a =receipt=; =GET /api/erasure/<receipt>= (no login needed) answers
  ={"state": "pending"}= or ={"state": "done"}=, and 404 for anything else. Only the receipt's sha256 is
  stored. The account's email is released at the 202, so the same address can sign up again immediately.
  A step that keeps failing logs =erasure stuck <step> <ErrorClass> attempts=<n>= after 10 attempts and
  again every 10 attempts (no ids); search the log for =erasure stuck=. The usual causes are a database
  that is down, a chat turn that will not park (=ErasureParkedError=), or a migration that narrowed a
  grant.
- Backups: keep database backups (Postgres dumps, WAL archives, the key-store rows) for at most 30 days.
  An erasure cannot reach a copy that already exists, so the copy's life is the erasure's last step. After
  restoring a backup, re-run erasures made since it was taken: insert their ids into =erasure_requests=
  as =pending= (the audit rows of the live database are the list) and start the server. A backup of the
  key store that outlives the 30 days defeats erasure for everyone in it; delete it on the same schedule.
  The blob directory is encrypted per user and needs the same schedule.
```

  and replace the sentence "Backup and erasure runbooks are listed in the spec as M7 work and do not exist yet." with "The backup and restore drill (restore into an empty database, verify, re-run erasures) is M7 work; the rules above are the ones that hold today."

- [ ] **Step 9: Check the links and the table.** `grep -n "complete" README.org` (expected: no match in the Status table), `grep -n "planned" README.org` (M6 and M7 only), and `grep -n "item 34\|^34\." docs/specs/2026-10-18-docmind-design.md`. Run `pnpm lint` (the docs are outside Biome, but a stray change to code fails it). Commit with `docs: erasure as built, the trade-offs, four cross-tenant paths, backups, status`. Open the PR.

## Execution notes

Probes are small checks the implementer makes where the plan could not run the code. Each has a written fallback, and none needs a Loom change.

| Task and step | Probe | Fallback |
|---|---|---|
| 164 step 6 | `ErasureWiring.raw.eventStore` accepts `CustomStorage["eventStore"]` | If `tsc` rejects it (the types differ only by the optional-method list), widen `ErasureWiring.raw.eventStore` to `DurableStorage["raw"]["eventStore"]` (import the type from `../storage`); do not cast |
| 164 step 7 | A liveness fold accepts the fabricated `NOTE_ADDED` log as non-terminal (only if the first test fails with outcome `parked`; read the failure's `pending` list) | Drop `NOTE_ADDED` and seal a path the fold knows: register `["output.value"]` for `STEP_COMPLETED` and write `{ stepId: "s", output: { value: text } }`; keep the assertions and record the shape in the PR body |
| 165a step 6 | `projection$` accepts a `PAYLOAD_SHREDDED` handler key | If the compile error names it, register the handler through a cast-free helper in `status/model.ts` (`const SHREDDED = "PAYLOAD_SHREDDED" as const;` as a computed key) and record the Loom type in the PR description |
| 165b step 7 | The second test rejects with `PayloadShreddedError` (the scan reached the erased chat first). If it rejects with `SensitiveStateError`, the sensitive map is not the production one | Build the map with `statusSensitive(flowSteps(), durable.sealed.eventStore)`; never hand-write it |
| 168a step 4 | The app role's `UPDATE` on `users` (the email scrub of Task 170a) works under the `tenant_isolation` policy; the 168a test "the app role can rewrite users.email" is the probe | If it fails, do the update inside an existing definer path or extend `0024` minimally; do not add a seventh definer function (fold it into the request-insert path) |
| 168a step 4 | RLS column qualification in the `0024` insert policy compiles | If `WITH CHECK` reports an ambiguous column, write `u.tenant_id = erasure_requests.tenant_id AND u.id = erasure_requests.user_id` and rerun |
| 171b step 6 | `Eraser.resumeAll` (called by `afterListen`) has finished before the assertion | The test awaits one more `resumeAll()` (idempotent) before `assertErased` |
| 171b step 11 | The running-turn test relies on the eraser's `stop` step aborting the held turn through the stop registry; it is the least deterministic test of the phase | If it hangs at `waitErased`, print the request's `steps`: a hang before `shred` means the stop signal did not reach the turn; a hang inside `shred` means the conversation did not park (each retry logs `ErasureParkedError`) |

Other things the implementer must know:

- **Not done by design:** `shredSubject`'s `reason` is not set (the erasure request is the audit record, D7). `hydrateSubjectIndex` runs once at boot. The in-flight race (a stream or upload that passed authentication just before `closeUser`) is closed by the heartbeat re-check on both streams and by the second `blobs` run in the `key` step.
- **Version bumps:** ingest-status `"3"` to `"4"` and chat-activity `"2"` to `"3"` re-fold both models at the first boot after the merge. Say so in the PR body of Task 165.
- **Rebuild residue:** a rebuild can re-create an erased execution's row as `{}` (key and scope only). The ingest-status copy is also swept by `docmind_orphan_statuses`; the chat-activity copy is re-forgotten at every boot by `Eraser.resumeAll`.
- **Formatting:** code blocks are written for readability and some lines exceed Biome's width; each task's lint step is `pnpm lint:fix && pnpm lint`.
- **Draft PRs and the gate:** every PR is a draft, rebase-merged, with `DOCMIND_TEST_PG_URL` exported in the same command as the gate.

## Self-review

**Spec coverage (spec section 9 item 34 and the D-decisions).**
- D1 (route, password confirm via `AuthService.confirmPassword`, throttle, one transaction, 202 with a receipt, cookie): Task 170a. D1a (the receipt route): Task 170b, with `docmind_erasure_status` in 168a and `receipt.ts` in 168b. D1b (the email scrub): one more statement in 170a's transaction, with the app role's `UPDATE` on `users` pinned by a 168a test. Login and session refusal of an erasing user stay in `0024` (SQL) as the second safeguard. Sign-up of the old email needs no code: the address is free after the scrub, so `auth_sign_up` creates a new account; `erasing.pg.test.ts` and 171b's gated test prove it, with the old row untouched.
- D2 (durable request, RLS with INSERT-only policy, definer functions, `erasing_at`, tenant function): Task 168; used by Tasks 169 and 170, proved by 171 (audit row asserted, `docmind_delete_empty_tenant` through the solo-tenant case). Definer count: six by name (four erasure functions and the sweep's two), unchanged by `erased()` (R1) and by the scrub (a plain `UPDATE`).
- D3 (seven steps, in order, idempotent, recorded; `key` re-runs `blobs`): Task 169a/b (`STEP_ORDER`, one test per rule); the step bodies are in `eraser.ts`, their SQL in `pgSteps.ts`.
- D4 (single flight map plus advisory lock, boot resume, `stop`, backoff, class-only logging, the stuck warning every 10 attempts): Tasks 169a/b; log hygiene is asserted in the unit tests (`TypeError` class, no email, no user id; the stuck line carries step, class and count only) and in 171a (every `console.*` call of a whole erasure scanned for ids, email and the secrets).
- D5 (boot safety): Task 165 builds it; 171b steps 5 and 6 prove it (restart over a shredded log, then a restart after the read models are thrown away, so every model refolds over shredded logs).
- D6 (personal tenant): Task 169b test (kept while a colleague remains) and 171a (gone for a solo user).
- D7 (what stays, including the `data_keys` tombstone row): Task 172 writes it down; 171a `assertErased` step 3 asserts it.
- D8, every bullet: 171a (`namingRows` over `information_schema`, tombstoned read-model rows only, no blob directory, `wasDestroyed`, raw log unreadable, sealed read refuses with a shredded error, no timers or leases) and 171b (neighbour, credentials, re-sign-up, re-run, two restarts with a forced rebuild, crash between `shred` and `rows`, plus a running turn).
- Cross-tenant paths now four: Task 172 step 1. Status table, backups at 30 days, `.env.example`: Task 172.

**Type consistency.**
- `ErasureRequest`, `ErasureStep`, `ErasureStore` (`insert(tx, userId, tenantId, receiptHash)`, `status(receiptHash)`, `pending`, `erased`, `mark`, `retried`, `get`) and `PgErasureStore(pool)` / `MemoryErasureStore` are used by Tasks 169, 170 and 171 as Task 168 defines them (`erasure/types.ts`, `pgStore.ts`, `testing.ts`). `erased()` is required in the interface, the Pg and memory stores, and `ErasureDeps.store`.
- `Eraser`: `run`, `resumeAll`, `idle`, `stop`. `ErasureDeps` is a flat set of functions so each step is testable on its own, including `eraseSubject(userId)`.
- `AccountErasure.request(identity, receiptHash)`, `start(userId)`, `status(receiptHash)`; `AuthRoutesDeps.erasure`; `ComposeOptions.erasure`; `startIngestStack({ wrapErasure })`; `IngestStack.eraser`. Names used in the tests match the definitions. The header's catalogue lists each.
- `STEP_ORDER` is the single order; `assertErased` compares the audit row's `steps` to it, so adding a step later fails the proof until the doc is updated.

**Rulings applied.**
- R1: `erased()` is required, not optional. It is `docmind_erasure_pending('done')`, so the definer count does not grow. `Eraser.resumeAll` calls it unconditionally after the models boot and re-runs `forgetUser` for each id. Covered by the 168a function tests (state validation, `'done'` listing), the `memoryStore` and `pgStore` tests for `erased()`, and the 169a `resumeAll` re-forget test.
- R2: 171b step 6 keeps the strict `live` query of `assertErased`. The restart and forced rebuild go through the real compose boot path, so `Eraser.resumeAll` re-tombstones whatever the refold re-created, and no live row of the erased user remains. The step awaits one more `resumeAll()` only to be deterministic.
- R3: `Eraser.stop()` and `stopErasure`, `ComposeOptions.erasure` seams, `ErasureDeps.eraseSubject`, the `key` step re-running blobs, the `data_keys` tombstone row in D7, `AuthService.confirmPassword`, and the stuck warning are all folded into the header's decisions and catalogue. R4 (receipt, stuck warning, email scrub, sixth definer function) replaces the earlier ruling that sign-up of an erasing email returns 202 and creates nothing.

**Known risks.**
1. `eraseSubject` is called with the tenant **undefined**: with a tenant id Loom's index answers `unknown` and writes no tombstones, silently. The shred step accepts `unknown` by design (a user with no executions), so only 171's `assertLogUnreadable` (tombstones present, sealed reads refused) would catch a regression.
2. Cascades under `docmind_app`: `DELETE FROM users` relies on the foreign-key actions running with the table owner's rights. If a later migration tightened a child table, `rows` fails and retries forever (visible as a stuck `rows` step and a growing `attempts` on the request). `pgSteps.pg.test.ts` covers `chat_sessions`; the e2e covers documents, chunks, entities and links.
3. Login and session refusal for an erasing user live in migration `0024` (SQL); Task 170a only tests them (the email scrub makes them a second safeguard). A session created in the instant between the route's transaction and a concurrent login is cascaded away by the `rows` step.
4. `settleStart` waits at most 5 s per document; a start that lands later is covered by the ingest flow's `document_gone` check (M3), not by the eraser.
5. 171b's running-turn test is the least deterministic in the phase (see Execution notes).
6. `resumeAll` runs un-awaited from `afterListen`; a failure in the `erased()` list or a `forgetUser` is logged by class and retried at the next boot, not retried in-process.
7. The receipt route is unauthenticated: the 256-bit token is the only credential, the per-IP throttle counts every request, and the answer is a state and nothing else. It confirms that a given erasure exists and how far it is; anyone who loses the token cannot recover it (only its hash is stored).
8. The stuck counter is per run of the eraser, so a restart restarts the count (the durable `attempts` column keeps the total). A shred parked behind a long chat turn warns too, which is intended.
